# Zettel

<details>

<summary>1930–1948, 725 remarks, Ts-233a, Ts-233b, Ts-232</summary>

<img class="viz" src="/viz/W-Z.svg">

</details>

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#1.1) <span class="fac">[1\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/1.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-11}

<span class="series-number">1</span> W. James: the thought is already complete at the beginning of the sentence. How can one know this? But the _intention_ to express it can already exist before the first word is said. For if one asks someone “do you know what you want to say?”, he will often answer in the affirmative.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#1.1.2) <span class="fac">[1\[1\].2](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/1.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-112}

<span class="series-number">2</span> I say to someone, “I will now whistle the tune…”, I have the intention to whistle it, & I already know what I will whistle. I have the intention to whistle this tune: does this mean that I have already whistled it in some sense, perhaps in thought?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#1.2) <span class="fac">[1\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/1.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-12}

<span class="series-number">3</span> “I do not merely _say_ this, I mean something by it.” – Should one ask “What?” – then another sentence is given as an answer. – Or can one not ask in this way, since the sentence says something like “I do not merely _say_ this, but it also moves me.”

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#1.3) <span class="fac">[1\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/1.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-13}

<span class="series-number">4</span> (One of the most misleading ways of speaking is the question “What do I mean by that?” – In most cases, one could answer: “Nothing at all – I _say_ …”)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#1.4) <span class="fac">[1\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/1.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-14}

<span class="series-number">5</span> Can I not express with words what I want? – Look at the door of your room, and while looking, say a series of arbitrary sounds, and mean by it a description of that door!

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#1.5) <span class="fac">[1\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/1.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-15}

<span class="series-number">6</span> “Say ‘a b c d’ and mean by it: The weather is nice.” – Am I to say, then, that uttering a sentence in a language familiar to us is a completely different experience than uttering sounds that are not familiar to us as a sentence? If I were to learn the language in which “a b c d” has that sense, would I gradually acquire the experience we are familiar with when uttering these letters? Yes & no. – A main difference between the two cases lies in the fact that in the first case I _cannot move_. It is as if one of my joints were in a cast, & I have not yet become accustomed to it & do not yet have the possible movements at my disposal, so that I constantly bump into it.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#2.1) <span class="fac">[2\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/2.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-21}

<span class="series-number">7</span> If I have two friends with the same name, & I write a letter to one of them; what is it that prevents me from writing it to the other? Is it the content? But it could fit for both. (I haven’t written the address yet.) Now, the connection could lie in the backstory. But then it also lies in what _follows_ the writing. If someone now asks me, “Which of the two are you writing to?” & I answer him, do I exclude the answer from the backstory? Don’t I give it almost as I say, “I have tooth pain”? Could I be in doubt about which of the two I am writing to? & what does such a case of doubt look like? – Yes, wouldn’t the case of a deception also be possible: I believe I am writing to one, & I write to the other? & what would the case of such a deception look like?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#2.2) <span class="fac">[2\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/2.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-22}

<span class="series-number">8</span> Sometimes one says: “What was I looking for in this drawer? – Oh yes, the photograph!” And when this occurs to us, we remember the connection of our action with what preceded it. But it could also be the case: I open the drawer & rummage in it; finally, I, as it were, come to my senses & ask myself, “Why am I searching in this drawer?” And then comes the answer, “I want to see the photograph of…” “I _want_,” not “I _wanted_.” The opening of the drawer, etc., happened, as it were, automatically & received an interpretation _later_.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#2.3) <span class="fac">[2\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/2.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-23}

<span class="series-number">9</span> “I wanted to get at _him_ with this remark.” If I hear that, I can imagine a situation and its history. I could stage it in a theater, put myself in the state of mind in which I want to ‘get at him’. – But how is this state of mind to be described? That is, how is it to be identified? – I imagine myself in the situation, adopt a certain facial expression and voice, etc. What connects my words with him? The situation and my thoughts. And my thoughts not otherwise than words that I utter.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#3.1) <span class="fac">[3\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/3.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-31}

<span class="series-number">10</span> Suppose I wanted to replace all the words in my language with other words at once; how could _I_ know where one of the new words is located? Are it the images that hold the places of the words?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#3.2) <span class="fac">[3\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/3.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-32}

<span class="series-number">11</span> I am inclined to say: I ‘point’ _in different senses_ to this body, to its shape, to its color, etc. – What does that mean? What does it mean: I ‘hear’ _in another sense_: the piano, its sound, the piece of music, the pianist, his skill? I ‘marry,’ in one sense, a woman, in another, her money.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#3.3) <span class="fac">[3\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/3.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-33}

<span class="series-number">12</span> Here, ‘meaning’ is imagined as a kind of mental pointing, indicating.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#3.4) <span class="fac">[3\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/3.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-34}

<span class="series-number">13</span> In some spiritualist actions, it is essential that one _think_ of a specific person. And we have here the impression that ‘thinking of him’ is as if one were, as it were, impaling him with my thoughts. Or it is as if I were repeatedly poking at him with thoughts. Because they keep drifting away from him.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#3.5) <span class="fac">[3\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/3.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-35}

<span class="series-number">14</span> “I suddenly had to think of him.” His image suddenly appeared before me. Did I know that it was the image of N? I didn’t say it to myself. So what was it that made it his image? Perhaps in what I said or did later.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#3.6) <span class="fac">[3\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/3.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-36}

<span class="series-number">15</span> If Max says “The prince exercises guardianship over the troops,” he means Wallenstein. – Suppose someone said: We don’t know if he means Wallenstein; he could also mean another prince in this sentence.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#3.7) <span class="fac">[3\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/3.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-37}

<span class="series-number">16</span> “The fact that you meant the piano playing consisted in the fact that you _thought_ of the piano playing.” “The fact that in this letter you meant this person with the word ‘you’ consisted in the fact that you wrote to _him_.” The mistake is: that ‘meaning’ does not consist in something.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#4.1) <span class="fac">[4\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/4.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-41}

<span class="series-number">17</span> “When I said that, I only wanted to give him a hint.” – How can I know that I only said it to give him a hint? Well, the words “When I said it, etc.” describe a situation that is understandable to us. What does the situation look like? To describe it, I must describe a connection.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#4.2) <span class="fac">[4\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/4.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-42}

<span class="series-number">18</span> How does _he_ enter into these processes: I poked at him, I spoke to him, I called him, I spoke about him, I imagined him, I pay attention to him?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#4.3) <span class="fac">[4\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/4.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-43}

<span class="series-number">19</span> It is wrong to say: I meant him _by_ looking at him. ‘Meaning’ is not used as a designation of an activity that consists wholly or partly in the ‘utterances’ of meaning.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#4.4) <span class="fac">[4\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/4.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-44}

<span class="series-number">20</span> It would therefore be silly to call it a ‘mental activity,’ because that would evoke a false idea of the function of the word.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#4.5) <span class="fac">[4\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/4.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-45}

<span class="series-number">21</span> I say “Come here!” and point in the direction of A. B, who is standing next to him, takes a step towards me. I say: “No; A should come.” Would one now regard this as a communication about my inner experiences? Certainly not. – And could one not still draw conclusions from it about processes that took place in me while uttering the command? But what kind of processes? Could one not assume that I looked at A while giving my command; that my train of thought led me to him? But perhaps I don’t know B at all; I am only connected with A. Then, whoever guessed at my mental processes could have been completely wrong, and yet would have understood that I meant A and not B.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#5.1) <span class="fac">[5\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/5.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-51}

<span class="series-number">22</span> I point with my hand and say “Come here!”. A asks, “Did you mean me?” I say, “No; I meant B.” – What was going on when I meant B (since my pointing made it doubtful which one I meant)? – I said these words, I made this hand gesture. Did anything else have to happen in order for the language-game to take place? But didn’t I already know who I meant while I was pointing? Did I know? For example, I could have pointed at B, even if A hadn’t been standing near him. Did I know? Of course – according to the usual criteria of knowledge.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#5.2) <span class="fac">[5\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/5.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-52}

<span class="series-number">23</span> “I wanted to steer my explanation toward…”. This goal was in my mind. I could see in my mind the place in the book that I was aiming for. To describe the intention means to describe what was going on, from a certain point of view, for a certain purpose. I paint a specific portrait of the events.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#5.3) <span class="fac">[5\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/5.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-53}

<span class="series-number">24</span> Instead of “I meant _him_,” one can also say, “I spoke _of_ him.” And how does one do that: speak _of_ him with these words? Why does it sound wrong to say, “I spoke of him _by_ pointing at him with these words”? “To mean him” means something like: to speak of him. Not: to point at him. And when I speak _of_ him, there is, of course, a connection between my speech and him, but this connection lies in the application of the speech, not in an act of pointing. The pointing itself is only a sign, and in the language-game it can regulate the application of the sentences, i.e., indicate what is meant.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#5.4) <span class="fac">[5\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/5.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-54}

<span class="series-number">25</span> If I say, “I saw an armchair in this room,” then I can usually only remember the specific visual image in a very casual way, and in most cases it also has no meaning. The use that is made of the sentence goes beyond this particularity. Is it the same when I say, “I meant N”? Does this sentence also go beyond the particularities of the event in the same way?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#6.1) <span class="fac">[6\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/6.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-61}

<span class="series-number">26</span> If I allude to N with a remark, this—given certain circumstances—may be discernible from my gaze, facial expression, etc.

The fact that you understand the expression “to allude to N” can be demonstrated by describing examples of allusion. What will you describe? Primarily, the circumstances. Then, what someone says. Perhaps also his gaze, etc. Then, what the person making the allusion wants to do. And if I also share my feelings, images, etc., with someone while making the remark, these may complete the typical image of the allusion (or _an_ image of this kind). But it does not follow from this that the expression “to allude to N” means: to behave in such a way, to feel this, to imagine this, etc. And here some will say: “Of course not! We have always seen that. And there must be a common thread running through all these phenomena. It is, as it were, interwoven with them, and therefore difficult to find.” – And that is not true either.

But it would also be wrong to say that “alluding” denotes a family of mental and other processes. – For one can ask “Which was your allusion to N?”, “How did you make it clear to the other person that you meant N?”; but not: “How did you intend this utterance as an allusion to N?” “I alluded to him in my speech.” – “With which words?” – “I alluded to him when I spoke of a man who…” “I alluded to him” means approximately: I _wanted_ someone to think of him when hearing these words. But “I wanted” is not a description of a state of mind, and “understanding that N was meant” is not either.

One does, however, ask: “With which remark did you allude to him?”, “With which remark did you mean him?”.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#6.2+8.1) <span class="fac">[6\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/6.webp),[8\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/8.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-6281}

<span class="series-number">27</span> If the situation is ambiguous, is it then doubtful whether I mean him? In my statement, “I meant him,” or “I did not mean him,” I do not judge according to the situation. And if I now do not judge according to the situation, according to what do I judge? Apparently, according to nothing. For I probably remember the situation, but I _interpret_ it. I can, for example, _reproduce_ my sidelong glance at him now, but the meaning seems to be a completely elusive, subtle atmosphere of speaking and acting. (A suspicious image!)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#8.2) <span class="fac">[8\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/8.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-82}

<span class="series-number">28</span> In the course of a conversation, I want to point to something; I have already begun a gesture of pointing; but I do not carry it out. Later, I say: “At that time, I wanted to point to it. I still clearly remember that I had already raised my finger.” In the stream of these events, thoughts, and sensations, this was the beginning of a gesture of pointing. Yes, if I made the whole gesture and said, “It is over there,” that would not be pointing, unless these words belonged to a language.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#8.3) <span class="fac">[8\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/8.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-83}

<span class="series-number">29</span> “You made a movement with your hand; did you mean something by it? – I thought you meant that I should come to you.” So he could have meant something, or he could have meant nothing. And if the former: then it was his hand movement, or something else? Did he mean something else with his expression, or did he only _mean_ his expression?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#8.4) <span class="fac">[8\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/8.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-84}

<span class="series-number">30</span> Could one also say: “I meant something with this movement, which I can only express through this movement”? (Music, musical thought.)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#8.5) <span class="fac">[8\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/8.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-85}

<span class="series-number">31</span> “Of course, I thought of him: I had him before my eyes!” – but I didn’t recognize him from his _image_.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#8.6+10.1) <span class="fac">[8\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/8.webp),[10\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/10.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-86101}

<span class="series-number">32</span> Imagine one of your acquaintances! – Now say, who it was! – Sometimes the image comes first, and the name later. But do I guess the name from the similarity of the image? – And if the name follows the image, was the image already there with the image, or was it only complete with the name? After all, I did not infer the name from the image; & that is why I can say that the image of him came with the image.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#10.3) <span class="fac">[10\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/10.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-103}

<span class="series-number">33</span> It is as if one experiences a _tendency_, a predisposition (James). And why should I not call it that? (And some would also explain what happens through innervation of muscles, approaches to movements, or even images of them.) Only, you must not see the experience of a tendency as the image of an incomplete experience.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#10.2) <span class="fac">[10\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/10.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-102}

It often seems to us as if the mind makes small movements in understanding the meaning, like someone who is undecided and does not know which way to go – he explores the area of possible applications.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#10.4) <span class="fac">[10\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/10.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-104}

<span class="series-number">34</span> Imagine people who, from childhood, scribble quickly while they talk, as if to illustrate what they are saying. Must I assume that whoever draws, describes, or imitates something from imagination or memory _reads off_ his representation from something?! – What speaks for it?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#10.5) <span class="fac">[10\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/10.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-105}

<span class="series-number">35</span> Guessing thoughts. Playing cards are lying on a table. I want the other person to touch one of them. I close my eyes and think of one of these cards; the other person should guess which one I mean. – He may think of a card and wish to guess my intention. He touches the card, and I say, “Yes, that was it,” or it was not. A variant of this game would be for me to _look_ at a specific card, in such a way that the other person does not see the direction of my gaze, and that he now has to guess which card I am looking at. The fact that this is a variant of the first game is important. It may be important _how_ I think of the card, because it may turn out that the reliability of the guessing depends on it. But in everyday life, if I say, “I was just thinking of N,”

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#12.2) <span class="fac">[12\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/12.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-122}

<span class="series-number">36</span> One might ask: “If someone were able to see into your inner self, would they be able to see that you _intended_ to say _that?_” Suppose I had written down my intention on a piece of paper; then another person could read my intention there. And can I imagine that he could have learned it in some other way, in a _more certain_ way? Certainly not.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#12.3) <span class="fac">[12\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/12.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-123}

<span class="series-number">37</span> (Above a piece of music, the composer has written <math display="inline"><mtext>♩</mtext></math> = 88, but in order to play it correctly today, it must be played at <math display="inline"><mtext>♩</mtext></math> = 94; which is _the tempo intended by the composer_?)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#12.4) <span class="fac">[12\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/12.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-124}

<span class="series-number">38</span> Interrupt a person in his completely unprepared and flowing speech. Then ask him what he wanted to say, and in many cases he will be able to continue the sentence he had begun. – “He must have already had in mind what he wanted to say.” – Is it not perhaps this phenomenon that is the reason why we say that the continuation was already in his mind?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#12.5) <span class="fac">[12\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/12.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-125}

<span class="series-number">39</span> But is it not strange that there is such a reaction, such a confession of intention? Is it not a very remarkable linguistic tool? What is actually remarkable about it? Well, it is difficult to imagine how a person learns this use of words. It is so subtle.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#12.6) <span class="fac">[12\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/12.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-126}

<span class="series-number">40</span> But is it really more subtle than the use of the words “I had imagined it,” for example? Yes, remarkable, strange, is any such use of language, if one is only focused on the consideration of descriptions of physical objects.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#12.7) <span class="fac">[12\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/12.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-127}

<span class="series-number">41</span> If I say “I wanted to do this and that at the time” and this statement is based on the thoughts, images, etc., that I remember, then another person, to whom I only communicate these thoughts, images, etc., should be able to draw the same conclusions from them with the same certainty that I wanted to do this and that at the time. – But he could often not. Yes, if I myself were to draw conclusions about my intention from this evidence, the other person would rightly say that this conclusion is very uncertain.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#14.1) <span class="fac">[14\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/14.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-141}

<span class="series-number">42</span> And how does one learn to use the expression “I was at that time in the process of throwing”? And how does one know that he was really in that state of mind that _I_ call “in the process of being…”?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#14.3) <span class="fac">[14\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/14.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-143}

<span class="series-number">43</span> Now, if a person never used the expression “I was at that time in the process of…” or “I wanted to…” and could not learn its use, what would happen? The person can think a great deal without thinking _that_. He can master a large area of language-games without mastering this one. But is it not strange that we do not encounter such defective people, despite all the differences between people? Or are these people to be found among the mentally deficient, and is it simply not observed closely enough which uses of language they are capable of and which they are not?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#14.4) <span class="fac">[14\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/14.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-144}

<span class="series-number">44</span> “I had the intention…” does not express the memory of an experience. (Just as little as “I was in the process of…”)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#14.5) <span class="fac">[14\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/14.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-145}

<span class="series-number">45</span> Intention is neither a feeling, mood, nor sensation, nor image. It is not a state of consciousness. It does not have real duration.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#14.6) <span class="fac">[14\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/14.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-146}

<span class="series-number">46</span> “I intend to travel tomorrow.” – When do you have the intention? All the time; or intermittently?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#15.1) <span class="fac">[15\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/15.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-151}

<span class="series-number">47</span> Look in the drawer where you think you can find it. The drawer is empty. – I suppose you looked for it among your sensations. Consider what it would actually mean to “have an intention intermittently.” It would mean something like: intending to drop it, pick it up again, and so on.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#15.2) <span class="fac">[15\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/15.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-152}

<span class="series-number">48</span> Under what circumstances would one say, “This device is a brake, but it doesn’t work”? This surely means: it does not fulfill its purpose. What is the reason that it has this purpose? One could also say: “The _intention_ was that this should function as a brake.” Whose intention? Here, the intention as a state of mind completely eludes us. Couldn’t one also imagine that several people have an intention, execute it, without any one of them having it? For example, a government can have an intention that no _person_ has.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#15.5) <span class="fac">[15\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/15.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-155}

<span class="series-number">49</span> There could be a verb that means: to express the intention in words, or other signs, aloud, or in thought. This verb would not be synonymous with our “intend.” There could be a verb that means: to act in accordance with an intention; and this would also not be synonymous with our “intend.” Another verb could mean: to brood over an intention; or, to turn it over and over in one’s head.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#15.6) <span class="fac">[15\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/15.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-156}

<span class="series-number">50</span> One can disturb someone in their thinking, – but in their intending? – – Perhaps in their planning. Also in holding fast to an intention, namely in thinking or acting.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#16.1) <span class="fac">[16\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/16.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-161}

<span class="series-number">51</span> Applications of the imperative. Compare the commands: Raise your arm! Imagine …! Calculate … in your head! Consider …! Concentrate your attention on …! See this figure as a cube! with these: Intend …! Mean it by these words …! Assume that this is the case! Believe that it is so! Be firmly convinced …! Remember that this happened! Doubt that it happened! Hope for its return!

Is _this_ the difference, that the former are voluntary, the latter involuntary movements of the mind? Rather, I can say that the verbs of the second group do not denote actions. (Compare this with the command: “Laugh heartily at this joke!”)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#16.2) <span class="fac">[16\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/16.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-162}

<span class="series-number">52</span> Can one command someone to understand a sentence? Why can’t one command someone: “Understand this!”? Could I not obey the command “Understand this Greek sentence!” by learning Greek? – – Similarly: One can say “Bring about pain!”, but not “Have pain!” One says: “Put yourself in this state!” but not: “Be in this state!”

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#7.1) <span class="fac">[7\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/7.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-71}

<span class="series-number">53</span> I expect an explosion every moment. I am unable to give my full attention to something else; I look at a book, but without reading. When asked why I seem distracted or nervous, I say that I expect an explosion every moment. – What was it like then: did this sentence describe just that behaviour? But how does the process of expecting an explosion differ from the process of expecting a completely different event, e.g. a specific signal? And how does the expectation of _one_ signal differ from the expectation of a slightly different signal? Or was my way of acting merely a side effect of the actual expectation, and was this a mental process? And was this process homogeneous, or structured like a sentence (with _internal_ beginning and end)? – But how does the one in whom it is happening know what event the process is the expectation of? He does not seem to be in doubt about it. It is not as if he were stating a mental or other state and making a guess about its cause. He might say “I do not know if it is only this expectation that is making me so restless today”; but he would not say: “I do not know if this state of mind I am now in is the expectation of an explosion, or of something else.” The statement “I expect an explosion every moment” is a _manifestation_ of the expectation. This verbal reaction is the deflection of the pointer that indicates what is expected.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#7.2) <span class="fac">[7\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/7.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-72}

<span class="series-number">54</span> It seems that: the expectation and the fact that satisfies the expectation somehow fit together. One might describe an expectation and a fact that fit together, so that one can see what this agreement consists of. One immediately thinks of the fitting of a solid into a _corresponding_ hollow form. But if one wants to describe these two, one sees that, insofar as they fit, _one_ description applies to both. (Compare this with what it means “These trousers don’t fit with this skirt”.)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#9.1) <span class="fac">[9\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/9.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-91}

<span class="series-number">55</span> As with all metaphysics, the harmony between thoughts and reality is to be found in the grammar of language.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#9.2) <span class="fac">[9\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/9.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-92}

<span class="series-number">56</span> My thought here is: If one could see the expectation itself – one would have to see _what_ is expected. (So that it would not require a method of projection, a method of comparison, to get from what one sees to the fact that is expected.) But that is also how it is: Whoever sees the expression of the expectation sees ‘what is expected’.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#9.3) <span class="fac">[9\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/9.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-93}

<span class="series-number">57</span> The thought that it is only the finding that shows us what we were looking for, that only the fulfilment of the wish shows us what we wished for, means judging the process in the same way as the symptoms of expectation or searching in another person. I see him restlessly pacing up and down in his room; then someone comes to the door, and he becomes calm and shows signs of satisfaction. And now I say “He apparently expected this person”.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#9.4) <span class="fac">[9\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/9.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-94}

<span class="series-number">58</span> We say that the expression of the expectation ‘describes’ the expected fact, and we think of it as an object or complex that appears as the fulfilment of the expectation. – But the expected one is not the fulfilment, but: that he comes. The mistake is deeply rooted in our language: We say “I expect him” and “I expect his coming” and “I expect that he will come”.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#9.5) <span class="fac">[9\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/9.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-95}

<span class="series-number">59</span> It is difficult for us to break away from the comparison: The person enters – the event enters. As if the event were already pre-formed in front of the door of reality and would now enter into it (as into a room).

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#9.6) <span class="fac">[9\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/9.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-96}

<span class="series-number">60</span> Reality is not a property that the expected one still lacks, and which now comes into being when the expectation comes about. – Reality is also not like daylight, which only gives things color when they are already present in the dark, as if colorless.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#9.7) <span class="fac">[9\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/9.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-97}

<span class="series-number">61</span> One can say of the bearer of a name that he does not exist; and that is of course not an activity, although one could compare it with one and say: he must be present if he does not exist. (And this has certainly already been written by a philosopher.)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#11.1) <span class="fac">[11\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/11.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-111}

<span class="series-number">62</span> The shadowy anticipation of the fact consists in the fact that we can now think that _that_ will happen, which will only happen _later_. Or, as it is misleadingly expressed: that we can now think of _that_ (or, to that), which will only happen _later_.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#11.2) <span class="fac">[11\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/11.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-112}

<span class="series-number">63</span> Someone might say, “Expectation is a thought.” This obviously corresponds to a use of the word “expect”. And we only want to remember that the process of thought can be _very varied_.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#11.3) <span class="fac">[11\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/11.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-113}

<span class="series-number">64</span> I whistle, and someone asks me why I am in good spirits. I reply, “I hope that N will come today.” – But while I was whistling, I was not thinking of him. And yet it would be wrong to say: I stopped hoping when I started whistling.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#11.4) <span class="fac">[11\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/11.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-114}

<span class="series-number">65</span> When I say “I expect…” – is this the statement: that the situation, my actions, thoughts, etc., are those of expecting this event; or do the words “I expect…” belong to the process of expectation? Under certain circumstances, these words will mean (can be replaced by) “I believe that this and that will happen”. Sometimes also: “Be prepared that…”.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#11.5) <span class="fac">[11\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/11.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-115}

<span class="series-number">66</span> The psychological – trivial – discussions about expectation, association, etc., always leave out the actually remarkable thing, and one can tell from them that they are beating around the bush without touching the crucial point.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#11.6+13.1) <span class="fac">[11\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/11.webp),[13\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/13.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-116131}

<span class="series-number">67</span> An expectation is embedded in a situation from which it arises. The expectation of an explosion, for example, can arise from a situation in which an explosion is _to be expected._ The person who expects it heard two people whispering: “Tomorrow at ten o’clock the fuse will be lit.” Then he thinks: perhaps someone here wants to blow up a house. Around ten o’clock he becomes restless, jumps at every noise, and finally answers the question of why he is nervous: “I am expecting…” This answer will, for example, make his behaviour understandable. It will also put us in a position to imagine his thoughts and feelings.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#13.2) <span class="fac">[13\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/13.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-132}

<span class="series-number">68</span> The fulfilment of the expectation does not consist in a third thing happening, which one could describe in some other way besides as “the fulfilment of this expectation,” such as, for example, as a feeling of satisfaction, or joy, or whatever. The expectation that something will be the case is the same as the expectation of the fulfilment of that expectation. Expectation of what is not.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#13.3) <span class="fac">[13\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/13.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-133}

<span class="series-number">69</span> Socrates to Theaetetus: “And he who imagines, should he not imagine _something_?” – Th.: “Necessarily.” – Soc.: “And he who imagines something, nothing real?” – Th.: “So it seems.”

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#13.4) <span class="fac">[13\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/13.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-134}

If we substitute the word “kill” for the word “imagine” in this argument, then there is a rule for the use of this word; it makes no sense to say “I kill something that does not exist.” I can imagine a deer on this meadow that is not there, but I cannot kill one that is not there. And “imagining a deer on this meadow” means: imagining that a deer is there. But killing a deer does not mean: killing that etc.. If, however, someone says “In order for me to imagine a deer, it must exist in a certain sense,” the answer is: no, it does not have to exist in any sense. And if it were answered: “But the brown color, for example, must exist in order for me to imagine it,” then one must say: “the brown color exists” does not mean anything at all; except perhaps that it is present here or there as the color of an object; and that is not necessary for me to imagine a brown deer.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#13.5) <span class="fac">[13\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/13.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-135}

<span class="series-number">70</span> Being able to do something appears like a shadow of the real doing, just as the sense of the sentence is a shadow of a fact, or the understanding of the command is a shadow of its execution. In the command, the fact, as it were, “casts its shadow in advance.” But this shadow, whatever it may be, is not the event.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#16.3) <span class="fac">[16\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/16.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-163}

<span class="series-number">71</span> Compare the applications of: “I have been in pain since yesterday.” “I have been expecting him since yesterday.” “I knew it since yesterday.” “I have been able to integrate since yesterday.”

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#16.4) <span class="fac">[16\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/16.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-164}

<span class="series-number">72</span> The common difference between all states of consciousness and the dispositions seems to me to be that one can convince oneself by taking samples whether they still persist.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#17.1) <span class="fac">[17\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/17.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-171}

<span class="series-number">73</span> One must read some sentences more than once in order to understand them as sentences.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#17.1.2) <span class="fac">[17\[1\].2](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/17.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-1712}

<span class="series-number">74</span> Let a sentence be given to me in code, along with its key; then, in a certain respect, everything necessary for understanding the sentence is naturally given to me. And yet, if asked “Do you understand this sentence?”, I would answer: No, not yet; I must first decipher it. And only when I have, for example, translated it into German, would I say “Now I understand it.” If one were to ask “At what moment in the translation do I now _understand_ the sentence?”, one would gain insight into the essence of what we call “understanding.”

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#17.2) <span class="fac">[17\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/17.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-172}

<span class="series-number">75</span> I can pay attention to the course of my pain; but not equally to that of my belief, or my knowledge.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#17.3) <span class="fac">[17\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/17.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-173}

<span class="series-number">76</span> One can determine the persistence of a phenomenon through uninterrupted observation, or through sampling. The observation of duration can be either uninterrupted or intermittent.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#17.3.2) <span class="fac">[17\[3\].2](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/17.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-1732}

<span class="series-number">77</span> How do you observe your knowledge, your opinions? And on the other hand, an image, a pain? Is there an uninterrupted observation of my ability to perform multiplication?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#17.4) <span class="fac">[17\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/17.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-174}

<span class="series-number">78</span> Is “I hope…” a description of a state of mind? A state of mind has a duration. “I have hoped all day…” is thus such a description. But if one says to another, “I hope you come” – how, if he asked me, “How long do you hope it?” Is the answer: “I hope, while I say it”? Suppose I had some answer to this question, would it not be quite irrelevant for the purpose of the words “I hope you will come”?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#17.5) <span class="fac">[17\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/17.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-175}

<span class="series-number">79</span> One says “I hope you will come,” but not “I believe: I hope you will come”; but it would be possible to say: “I believe, I still hope he will come.”

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#18.1) <span class="fac">[18\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/18.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-181}

<span class="series-number">80</span> What is the past tense of “Not true, you are coming!”?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#18.2) <span class="fac">[18\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/18.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-182}

<span class="series-number">81</span> Where there is genuine duration, one can say to another: “Pay attention and give me a sign when the image, the sound, etc., changes. There is, in fact, a paying of attention. Whereas one cannot track the forgetting of what one knows, and so on, with attention. (This is not true, for one cannot track one’s own mental images with attention either.)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#18.3) <span class="fac">[18\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/18.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-183}

<span class="series-number">82</span> Think of the language-game: Determine with the stopwatch how long the impression lasts. One could not, however, determine the duration of knowledge, skill, or understanding in this way.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#18.4) <span class="fac">[18\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/18.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-184}

<span class="series-number">83</span> “But the difference between knowledge and hearing does not simply lie in such a characteristic as the nature of their duration. They are quite fundamentally different!” Indeed. But one cannot simply say: “_Know_ and _hear_, and you will notice the difference!”

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#18.5) <span class="fac">[18\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/18.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-185}

<span class="series-number">84</span> “Pain is a state of consciousness, understanding is not.” – “Well, I simply do not _feel_ understanding.” – But this explanation doesn’t work. It also wouldn’t be an explanation (to say): What one in some sense _feels_ is a state of consciousness. That would only mean: State of consciousness = feeling. (One would only have replaced one word with another.)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#18.6) <span class="fac">[18\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/18.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-186}

<span class="series-number">85</span> in fact, it is hardly ever the case that one has believed, understood, or intended something “uninterruptedly” since yesterday. An interruption of belief would be a time of disbelief, not, for example, turning one’s attention away from what one believes, such as sleep. (Difference between ‘knowing’ and ‘being aware of’.)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#20.8) <span class="fac">[20\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/20.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-208}

(Difference between ‘knowing’ and being aware [unreadable].)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#19.1) <span class="fac">[19\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/19.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-191}

<span class="series-number">86</span> The most important thing here is that one can be conscious of a difference that is categorical, without being able to say what the difference consists in. This is the case where one usually says that one recognizes the difference through introspection.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#19.2) <span class="fac">[19\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/19.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-192}

<span class="series-number">87</span> This is probably the point at which one says that one can only communicate the form to the _other_, but not the content. – So one is essentially talking to oneself _about the content!_ – – (But how do my words relate to the content of which I am conscious, & for what purpose?)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#19.2.2) <span class="fac">[19\[2\].2](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/19.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-1922}

<span class="series-number">88</span> It is very remarkable that the _processes_ involved in thinking rarely interest us. It is remarkable, but not odd.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#19.3) <span class="fac">[19\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/19.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-193}

<span class="series-number">89</span> [Thoughts, as if only hints.] Is he not like a calculating machine here? – He has calculated correctly if the correct result comes out. He may not be able to say what was going on in him. And if we heard it, it might seem like a strange _caricature_ of a calculation.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#19.4) <span class="fac">[19\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/19.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-194}

<span class="series-number">90</span> What do I know about the inner processes of someone who reads a sentence with attention? And can he describe them to me after he has done so; and is what he describes perhaps the characteristic process of attention?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#19.5+20.1) <span class="fac">[19\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/19.webp),[20\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/20.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-195201}

<span class="series-number">91</span> Question: What effect do I want to achieve when I tell someone, “Read attentively!”? Perhaps that he notices this and that, and can report on it. – Again, I think one could say that when someone reads a sentence attentively, he will often be able to report on processes in his mind, perhaps images. But that does not mean that these processes are called “attention.”

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#20.2) <span class="fac">[20\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/20.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-202}

<span class="series-number">92</span> “Did you read the sentence thoughtfully?” – “Yes, I read it thoughtfully; every word was important to me.” That is not the usual experience. One does not usually listen to someone speaking in a half-surprised way; one does not follow one’s own speech with attention; for one usually speaks arbitrarily, not involuntarily.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#20.3) <span class="fac">[20\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/20.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-203}

<span class="series-number">93</span> If an otherwise normal person is having a normal conversation under normal circumstances, & I were asked how, in such a case, the person engaged in _Denken_ differs from someone not engaged in it, I wouldn’t know how to answer. & I certainly couldn’t say that the difference lies in something that happens, or doesn’t happen, while speaking.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#20.4) <span class="fac">[20\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/20.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-204}

<span class="series-number">94</span> The dividing line between ‘thinking’ and ‘not thinking’ that would be drawn here would run between two states that differ in nothing that even remotely resembles a play of images. (For the play of images remains the model according to which one wants to imagine thinking.)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#20.5) <span class="fac">[20\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/20.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-205}

<span class="series-number">95</span> Only under quite specific circumstances does the question arise as to whether one has spoken in a way that involves _thinking_, or not.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#20.5.2) <span class="fac">[20\[5\].2](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/20.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-2052}

<span class="series-number">96</span> Yes, if one speaks of an _experience_ of thinking, then the experience of speaking is as good as any other. But the concept ‘thinking’ is not a concept of experience. For one does not compare thoughts as one compares experiences.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#20.6) <span class="fac">[20\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/20.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-206}

<span class="series-number">97</span> What one imitates is, for example, the tone of speech, the facial expression, and so on, and that is enough. This proves that _here_ the important accompanying phenomena of speech lie.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#20.7) <span class="fac">[20\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/20.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-207}

<span class="series-number">98</span> Let’s say that _everyone_ who speaks meaningfully thinks? E.g., the builder in language-game No. 2? Could we not imagine the building and calling out of words, etc., in an environment in which we would not associate it with thinking in the slightest?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#21.1) <span class="fac">[21\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/21.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-211}

<span class="series-number">99</span> [Regarding language-game No. 2] “You are, of course, tacitly assuming that these people _think_; that in _this_ respect they are the same as the people known to us; that they do not perform this language-game in a purely mechanical way. For if you imagine that they do, then you would not even call it the use of a rudimentary language.” What should I answer to that? It is of course true that the life of these people must resemble ours in many respects, and I have said nothing about these similarities. But the important thing is that their language, as well as their thinking, may be primitive, that there is a ‘primitive thinking’ that can be described by a primitive _behaviour_. The environment is not the ‘thinking accompaniment’ of speech.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#21.2+22.1) <span class="fac">[21\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/21.webp),[22\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/22.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-212221}

<span class="series-number">100</span> Let us imagine that someone is performing a task involving comparisons, trials, and choices. He is, for example, constructing an object from certain pieces of material using given tools. Again and again, the problem arises: “Should I take _this_ piece?” – The piece is rejected, another is tried. Pieces are provisionally put together and taken apart; a suitable one is sought, and so on. I now imagine this entire process filmed. The worker also makes sounds, such as “Hm” or “Ha!” – sounds that might be described as expressions of hesitation, sudden discovery, decision, satisfaction, or dissatisfaction. But no words are spoken. These sounds may be recorded in the film. The film is shown to me, and I now invent an internal monologue of the worker, which fits his method of working, the rhythm of his work, his facial expressions, his gestures, and his natural sounds, which corresponds to all of this. I have him say, for example, “No, this piece is too long; perhaps another would fit better.” – – Or “What should I do now?” – “I’ve got it!” – – Or “This is quite good,” etc. If the worker could speak – would it be a distortion of the actual process if he described it accurately and said, for example, “Then I thought: No, that won’t work; I must try it differently,” and so on – even though he did not speak during the work and did not even have these words in mind? I mean, can he not reproduce his wordless thoughts later in words? In such a way that we, who saw the working process, could agree with this reproduction? – Especially if we had observed the man at work not just once, but several times?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#22.2) <span class="fac">[22\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/22.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-222}

<span class="series-number">101</span> We could, of course, not separate ‘thinking’ from the activity. Thinking is not simply an accompaniment to work; just as little as is thinking speech.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#22.3) <span class="fac">[22\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/22.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-223}

<span class="series-number">102</span> 185 If we were to observe their essence at work, their working **rhythm**, their facial expressions, etc., and if these were similar to ours, except that they did not _speak_, then we might say that they were thinking, deliberating, making decisions. There would, of course, be _much_ that corresponded to the actions of ordinary people. And it is not possible to determine _how_ exact this correspondence must be in order for us to have the right to apply the concept of ‘thinking’ to them as well.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#22.4) <span class="fac">[22\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/22.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-224}

<span class="series-number">103</span> And what purpose would it serve to make this distinction? We will make an important distinction between those essences that can learn to perform a task, even a complicated one, ‘mechanically,’ and those that try and compare while working. ‒ ‒ However, what it is to call something “trying” and “comparing” can only be explained again by means of examples, and these examples will be taken from our life, or one that is similar to ours.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#23.1) <span class="fac">[23\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/23.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-231}

<span class="series-number">104</span> If he has, perhaps playfully or by chance, created a combination and now uses it as a method to do this and that, then we will say that he is thinking. – When _considering_ something, he would let means and ways pass before his mind's eye. But to do this, he must already have some in stock. Thinking gives him the possibility of _perfecting_ his methods. Or rather: he ‘thinks’ when he perfects his methods in a certain way. What does research look like?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#23.2) <span class="fac">[23\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/23.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-232}

<span class="series-number">105</span> One could also say: he thinks when he learns in a certain way.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#23.3) <span class="fac">[23\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/23.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-233}

<span class="series-number">106</span> And also this (one might say): Whoever _thinks_ while working will perhaps incorporate _auxiliary activities_ into it. The word “thinking” now does not refer to these auxiliary activities, just as thinking is not the same as speaking. Although the concept ‘thinking’ is formed in the manner of an imaginary auxiliary activity. (Just as one might say that the concept of the differential quotient is formed in the manner of an imaginary quotient.)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#23.4) <span class="fac">[23\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/23.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-234}

<span class="series-number">107</span> These auxiliary activities are not the thinking; but one imagines thinking as that which must flow beneath the surface of these aids, if they are not to be merely mechanical actions.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#23.5) <span class="fac">[23\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/23.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-235}

<span class="series-number">108</span> Suppose it concerns beings (human-like animals) that we use as slaves, that we buy & sell. They cannot learn to speak, but one can train the more gifted among them to often quite complicated tasks; & some of these perform their tasks ‘while thinking,’ others merely mechanically. We pay more for a thinking one than for one who is merely mechanically skilled.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#24.1) <span class="fac">[24\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/24.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-241}

<span class="series-number">109</span> If there were only a very few people who could find the answer to an arithmetic problem without speaking or writing, one could not cite them as testimony that one can also calculate without signs. Because it would not be clear that these people are ‘calculating’ at all. Similarly, the testimony of Ballard (James) cannot convince one that one can think without language. Indeed, why should one speak of ‘thinking’ where no language is used? If one does, that simply shows something about the _concept_ of thinking.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#24.2) <span class="fac">[24\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/24.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-242}

<span class="series-number">110</span> ‘Thinking,’ a widely branching concept. A concept that encompasses many manifestations of life. The **phenomena** of thought are widely divergent.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#24.3) <span class="fac">[24\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/24.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-243}

<span class="series-number">111</span> We are not at all _prepared_ for the task of describing the use of the word “thinking,” for example. (And why should we be? What is the point of such a description?) And the naive image that one forms of it does not at all correspond to reality. We expect a smooth, regular contour, and we see a ragged one. Here, one could really say that we have formed a false image. – It is almost as if there were a remark about a fragment.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#24.4) <span class="fac">[24\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/24.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-244}

<span class="series-number">112</span> It is not to be expected that this word has a uniform use; rather, it is to be expected that it does not.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#24.5) <span class="fac">[24\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/24.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-245}

<span class="series-number">113</span> Where do we get the concept of ‘thinking’ that we want to consider here? From everyday language. What first directs our attention is the word “thinking.” But the use of this word is confused. And we could not expect it to be otherwise. And this can naturally be said of all psychological verbs. Their use is not as clear, and as easy to overlook, as that of the words of mechanics, for example.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#25.3) <span class="fac">[25\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/25.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-253}

<span class="series-number">114</span> One learns the word “to think”, that is, its use, under certain circumstances, which, however, one does not learn to describe.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#25.1) <span class="fac">[25\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/25.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-251}

<span class="series-number">115</span> But I _can_ teach a person the _use_ of a word! because describing those circumstances is not necessary for that.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#25.2) <span class="fac">[25\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/25.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-252}

<span class="series-number">116</span> I am simply teaching him the phrase _under certain circumstances_.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#25.4) <span class="fac">[25\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/25.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-254}

<span class="series-number">117</span> One learns it only in connection with human beings, one asserts it of them, or denies it. The question “Does a fish think?” need not exist within the realm of its linguistic applications, it need _not be posed_. (What could be more natural than such a state; such a use of language?)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#25.5) <span class="fac">[25\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/25.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-255}

<span class="series-number">118</span> “No one thought of _this_ case” – one can say. I cannot, of course, enumerate the conditions under which the word “thinking” is in use, – but, if a circumstance makes its use doubtful, I can say so, and also, _how_ the situation deviates from the usual one.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#25.6) <span class="fac">[25\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/25.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-256}

<span class="series-number">119</span> If I have learned to perform a certain activity in a certain room (e.g., tidying up the room) & have mastered this technique, it does not follow that I must be prepared to describe the room’s furnishings; even if I were to immediately notice & be able to describe every change in them.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#25.7) <span class="fac">[25\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/25.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-257}

<span class="series-number">120</span> “This law was not enacted with such cases in mind. Is it therefore senseless?”

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#25.8+26.1) <span class="fac">[25\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/25.webp),[26\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/26.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-258261}

<span class="series-number">121</span> It is quite possible that someone knows a city very well, i.e., can find the shortest route from any place in the city to any other place with certainty, – and is yet quite incapable of drawing a map of the city. As soon as he tries, he only produces _utterly false_ results. (Our concept of ‘instinct’.)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#26.2) <span class="fac">[26\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/26.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-262}

<span class="series-number">122</span> One could have different words: for ‘thinking aloud’; for thinking in speech within one’s imagination; for pausing, while something is in one’s mind, after which, however, one can confidently give the answer. A word for the thought expressed in the sentence; one for the flash of thought that I can later ‘put into words’; one for thinking without words.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#26.3) <span class="fac">[26\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/26.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-263}

<span class="series-number">123</span> “Thinking is a mental activity.” – – Thinking is _no_ physical activity. Is thinking an activity? Well, one can command someone, “think about it!” But if someone, in obedience to this command, speaks to himself or also to another, is he performing _two_ activities?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#26.4) <span class="fac">[26\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/26.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-264}

<span class="series-number">124</span> Participation in what is said has its specific signs. It also has its specific consequences and preconditions. Participation is an experienced thing, and one does not say it of oneself on the basis of an observation. It is not an accompaniment of what is said. What would be, accompanying the sentence, a participation in the content of that sentence? (Logical condition)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#26.5) <span class="fac">[26\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/26.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-265}

<span class="series-number">125</span> Compare the phenomenon of thinking with the phenomenon of burning! Can not the burning, the flame, seem mysterious to us? And why the flame more than the table? – And how do you solve this riddle? And how is the riddle of thinking to be solved? – How is that of the flame?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#26.6) <span class="fac">[26\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/26.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-266}

<span class="series-number">126</span> Is the flame not mysterious because it is intangible? Probably – but why does that make it mysterious? Why should the intangible be more mysterious than the tangible? Except because we _want_ to grasp it. –

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#27.1) <span class="fac">[27\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/27.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-271}

<span class="series-number">127</span> It is said that the mind _leaves_ the body. But then every similarity between the mind & the body is removed, so that one certainly does not think that it is meant to be some kind of gaseous thing. It is said that this mind is incorporeal, non-spatial; but with the word “leaves” one has already said everything. Show me _how_ you use the word “psychical”, & I will see whether the mind is “incorporeal”, & what you understand by “spirit”.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#27.2) <span class="fac">[27\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/27.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-272}

<span class="series-number">128</span> I am inclined to speak of the inanimate as something that lacks something. I see life as an absolute plus, as something added to the inanimate. (Psychological atmosphere.)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#27.3) <span class="fac">[27\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/27.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-273}

<span class="series-number">129</span> One does not say of the table and chair: “it is thinking now,” nor “it is not thinking now,” nor “it never thinks”; also not of the plant, also not of the fish, hardly of the dog; but of the human being. And not of all human beings. “A table does not think” is not a statement like “A table does not grow.” (I don’t even know what it would be like if a table thought.) And here there is obviously a gradual transition to the case of the human being.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#27.4) <span class="fac">[27\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/27.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-274}

<span class="series-number">130</span> One only speaks of ‘thinking’ under quite specific circumstances.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#27.5) <span class="fac">[27\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/27.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-275}

<span class="series-number">131</span> How can the sense and the truth (or the truth and the sense) of the sentences collapse at the same time? (Stand and fall together?)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#27.6) <span class="fac">[27\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/27.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-276}

<span class="series-number">132</span> And is it not as if you wanted to say: “If it is as so and so, then it makes no sense to say that it is as so”?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#27.7+28.1) <span class="fac">[27\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/27.webp),[28\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/28.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-277281}

<span class="series-number">133</span> For example: “If one had _always_ made the wrong move, it would be pointless to speak of a ‘wrong move’.” But that is just a paradoxical way of putting it. The non-paradoxical form would be: “The general description… has no sense.”

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#28.2) <span class="fac">[28\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/28.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-282}

<span class="series-number">134</span> Instead of “one cannot,” say: “there is no such thing in this game.” Instead of “one cannot castle in the game of draughts” – “there is no castling in draughts”; instead of “I cannot demonstrate my sensation” – “there is no demonstrating what one has in the use of the word ‘sensation’”; instead of “one cannot enumerate all the cardinal numbers” – “there is no enumerating all the elements here.”

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#28.3) <span class="fac">[28\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/28.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-283}

<span class="series-number">135</span> The conversation, the use & interpretation of words flows along, & only in this flow does the word have its meaning. “He has left.” – “Why?” – What did you mean when you uttered the word “why”? What were you _thinking_ about?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#28.4) <span class="fac">[28\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/28.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-284}

<span class="series-number">136</span> Think of pointing in school. Must one have silently recited the answer to oneself in order to be entitled to point? And _what_ must have taken place in him for this? – Nothing. But it is important that he usually knows an answer when he points; & that is the criterion for him _understanding_ the pointing. Nothing must have taken place in him; & yet he would be strange who could report something about inner processes in such a case.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#28.5+29.1) <span class="fac">[28\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/28.webp),[29\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/29.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-285291}

<span class="series-number">137</span> Sometimes, when I say “I thought at that time…”, I can report that I had just said these very words aloud, or silently; or if not these, then other words, of which the present ones are a close **rendering**. That does happen sometimes! But also this, that my present words are ‘not a rendering’. For they are only a rendering if they are so according to the rules of depiction.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#29.2) <span class="fac">[29\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/29.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-292}

<span class="series-number">138</span> It seems as if a sentence that, for example, contains the word “sphere,” already contains the shadow of other uses of this word. Namely, the _possibility_ of forming those other sentences. – To whom does it seem so, and under what circumstances?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#29.3) <span class="fac">[29\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/29.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-293}

<span class="series-number">139</span> One cannot escape the fact that the sense of the sentence accompanies the sentence; it inheres in the sentence.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#29.4) <span class="fac">[29\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/29.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-294}

<span class="series-number">140</span> One might say: “one negation _does_ the same thing to the sentence as the other – it reverses it”. But these are just other words for equating the two negative sentences (which only holds if the negated sentence is not itself a negative sentence). Again and again, the thought that what one sees in the sign is only an exterior to an interior, within which the actual operations of sense and meaning take place.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#29.5) <span class="fac">[29\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/29.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-295}

<span class="series-number">141</span> Our problem could be stated as follows: _(very clearly)_ Suppose we have two systems of measuring lengths; in both, a length is expressed by a numerical sign followed by a word indicating the measure. One system describes a length as “n feet” and “foot” is a unit of length in the ordinary sense; in the other system, a length is described as “n W” and 1 foot = 1 W. But 2 W = 4 feet, 3 W = 9 feet, etc.—Thus, the sentence “This stick is 1 W long” means the same as “This stick is 1 foot long”. Question: Do “W” and “foot” have the same meaning in these two sentences?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#29.6+30.1) <span class="fac">[29\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/29.webp),[30\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/30.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-296301}

<span class="series-number">142</span> The question is posed incorrectly. This becomes clear if we express the equality of meaning through an equation. The question can then only be: “Is W = foot, or not?” – The sentences in which these signs appear disappear in this consideration. – Likewise, one cannot, of course, ask in this terminology whether “is” here means the same as “is” there; but rather, whether the copula means the same as the sign of equality. Now, we did say: 1 foot = 1 W; but foot ≠ W.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#30.2) <span class="fac">[30\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/30.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-302}

<span class="series-number">143</span> One could say: in all cases, when one speaks of a “thought,” one means the _living_ aspect of the sentence. That which, without it, is dead, a mere sequence of sounds or a sequence of written figures. But if one were to speak of something that gives meaning to a configuration of chess pieces, i.e., distinguishes it from an arbitrary arrangement of wooden blocks, what could one not mean! The rules that make the chess configuration a situation in a game; the particular experiences that we associate with such game positions; the use of the game. Or if we were to speak of something that distinguishes paper money from mere printed slips of paper and gives it its meaning, its life!

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#30.3) <span class="fac">[30\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/30.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-303}

<span class="series-number">144</span> How a word is understood, that words alone cannot say. (Theology.)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#30.4) <span class="fac">[30\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/30.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-304}

<span class="series-number">145</span> We can imagine a language in which, in its use, the impression we receive from the signs does not play a role; in which there is no understanding in the sense of such an impression. The signs are perhaps transmitted to us in writing, and we can _memorize_ _them_. (That is, the only impression that is being talked about is the image of the sign.) If it is now a command, then we translate the sign into action according to rules, tables. There is no impression similar to that of a picture, and one does not write stories in this language.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#31.1) <span class="fac">[31\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/31.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-311}

<span class="series-number">146</span> (Regarding No. 403.) In this case, could one say: “The sequence of signs is _dead_ without the system”?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#30.5) <span class="fac">[30\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/30.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-305}

<span class="series-number">147</span> It would, of course, also be conceivable that we would have to translate a sentence of word-language into a drawn picture in order to receive an _impression_ from it. (That it is only this picture that has a mind.)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#31.2) <span class="fac">[31\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/31.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-312}

<span class="series-number">148</span> One could imagine a language in which the meanings of words alternate according to certain rules, for example: in the morning, the word A means this, in the afternoon it means that. Or a language in which the words change daily, with each day every letter of the previous day being replaced by the next in the alphabet (and z by a).

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#31.3) <span class="fac">[31\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/31.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-313}

<span class="series-number">149</span> Imagine this language: the words and grammar are those of German, but the words in the sentence are in the opposite order. A sentence in this language therefore sounds like a German sentence that is read from the period to the beginning. The possibilities of expression therefore have the same variety as in German. But what we know as the sound of a sentence is destroyed.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#31.4) <span class="fac">[31\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/31.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-314}

<span class="series-number">150</span> Someone who does not speak German hears me exclaim on certain occasions: “What wonderful lighting!” He guesses the meaning and use, now the exclamation itself, as I do, without, however, understanding the three words. Does he understand the exclamation?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#31.5) <span class="fac">[31\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/31.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-315}

<span class="series-number">151</span> I had intentionally chosen an example in which a person gives expression to a sensation. For in this case, one says that sounds, which do not belong to any language, are full of meaning.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#31.5.2) <span class="fac">[31\[5\].2](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/31.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-3152}
