<span class="series-number">152</span> Would it be equally easy to imagine the analogous case for this sentence: “If the train does not arrive on time at 5 o’clock, it will miss its connection”? What, for example, would it mean in this case to guess at the sense?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#31.6) <span class="fac">[31\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/31.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-316}

<span class="series-number">153</span> It somehow bothers us that the thought of a sentence is never fully present at any moment. We look at it like an object that we create, and never fully possess, because as soon as one part arises, another disappears.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#32.1) <span class="fac">[32\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/32.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-321}

<span class="series-number">154</span> (Referring to No. 347.) One can easily imagine a language in which people use a single word for that exclamation. But what about a word for the sentence “If the train doesn’t arrive punctually at 5 o’clock”? In what case would we say that the word actually stands for this sentence? Perhaps in this case: People initially used a sentence like ours; then, however, circumstances arose in which the sentence had to be uttered so frequently that they condensed it into a word. These people could then still explain the word by means of the sentence. But is it also possible for there to be a case in which people have _only_ a word for that sense, that is, for that use? Why not? One must imagine how someone learns the use of this word, and under what circumstances we would say that the word actually represents that sentence. But consider this: In our language, someone says “It arrives at 5 o’clock”; another replies “No, 10 minutes past 5.” Is this type of conversation also possible in the other language? That is why sense and meaning are vague concepts.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#32.2) <span class="fac">[32\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/32.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-322}

<span class="series-number">155</span> The words of a poet can deeply affect us. And this is, _causally_, of course, related to the use they have in our lives. And it is also related to the fact that, according to this use, we let our thoughts wander here and there in the familiar environment of the words.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#32.3) <span class="fac">[32\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/32.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-323}

<span class="series-number">156</span> Is there _one_ kind of meaning that can be explained, and another kind that does not come to light in an explanation?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#32.4) <span class="fac">[32\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/32.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-324}

<span class="series-number">157</span> The soulful expression in music – it cannot be recognized by rules. And why can we not imagine that it is for other beings?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#32.5) <span class="fac">[32\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/32.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-325}

<span class="series-number">158</span> When suddenly “a theme, a turn, something” strikes you, you don’t need to be able to explain it. It is suddenly also accessible to you _as_ this gesture.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#35.6) <span class="fac">[35\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/35.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-356}

<span class="series-number">159</span> You are talking about _understanding_ music. You understand it _while_ you are listening to it! Should we say that it is an experience that accompanies listening?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#35.7) <span class="fac">[35\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/35.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-357}

<span class="series-number">160</span> The speaking of music. Don’t forget that a poem, even if written in the language of communication, is not used in the language-game of communication.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#35.7+36.1) <span class="fac">[35\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/35.webp),[36\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/36.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-357361}

<span class="series-number">161</span> Could one not imagine that someone who has never known music and comes to us and hears someone play a thoughtful Chopin piece would be convinced that this is a language and that someone is only concealing the meaning from him? In word-language, there is a strong musical element. (A sigh, the tone of the question, the announcement, the longing, all the countless _gestures_ of the tone.)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#33.1) <span class="fac">[33\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/33.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-331}

<span class="series-number">162</span> When I listen to a melody with understanding, is there not something special going on in me – something that does not happen when I listen to it without understanding? And _what_? – There is no answer; or what occurs to me is banal. I can say, “Now I have understood it” and then perhaps talk about it, play it, compare it with others, etc. _Signs_ of understanding may accompany the listening.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#33.2) <span class="fac">[33\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/33.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-332}

<span class="series-number">163</span> It is wrong to call understanding a process that accompanies listening. (One could also not call the expression of it, the expressive play, an accompaniment of listening.)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#33.3) <span class="fac">[33\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/33.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-333}

<span class="series-number">164</span> For how can one explain what “expressive play” is? Certainly not through something that accompanies the play. – So what does it consist of? One might say: a culture. – If someone has been raised in a particular culture, and thus reacts to music in a certain way, then one can teach him the use of the word “expressive play.”

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#33.4) <span class="fac">[33\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/33.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-334}

<span class="series-number">165</span> Understanding music is neither a sensation nor a sum of sensations. But calling it an experience is nevertheless correct in the sense that _this_ concept of understanding has certain affinities with other concepts of experience. One says, “I experienced this passage quite differently this time.” But nevertheless, this expression ‘describes’ ‘what happened’ only for someone who is familiar with a particular system of concepts. (Analogy: “I won the game.”)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#33.5+34.1) <span class="fac">[33\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/33.webp),[34\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/34.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-335341}

<span class="series-number">166</span> When reading, _that_ floats before me. So something is happening when reading…? – This question does not lead anywhere.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#34.2) <span class="fac">[34\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/34.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-342}

<span class="series-number">167</span> How can that float before me? – Not in the dimensions you are thinking of.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#34.3) <span class="fac">[34\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/34.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-343}

<span class="series-number">168</span> How do I know that someone is delighted? How does one learn the linguistic expression of delight? What does it attach to? To the expression of bodily sensations? Do we ask someone what he feels in his chest, in the facial muscles, in order to find out whether he is experiencing pleasure?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#34.4+35.1) <span class="fac">[34\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/34.webp),[35\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/35.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-344351}

<span class="series-number">169</span> Does that mean there aren’t sensations that often recur when enjoying music? Not at all.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#34.4+35.1.2) <span class="fac">[34\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/34.webp),[35\[1\].2](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/35.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-3443512}

<span class="series-number">170</span> A poem makes an _impression_ on us while we are reading it. “Do you feel the same thing while reading it as when you read something indifferent?” – How have I learned to answer this question? I will perhaps say: “Of course not!” – which means something like: this affects me, and the other does not. “I experience something different in the process.” – And what is the nature of this? – I cannot give a satisfactory answer. For what I state is nothing important. – “But did you not enjoy it _during_ the reading?” Certainly – – For the opposite answer would mean: I enjoyed it earlier, or later; and that is not what I want to say. But now you do remember sensations and images while reading, and indeed such ones that are connected with enjoyment, with the impression – But, they only obtained their importance through their context: through the reading of the poem, through my _familiarity_ with the language, the meter and _countless_ other connections. You must ask yourself, how did we ever learn the expression “Isn’t that wonderful!”? – No one explained it to us by referring to sensations, images, or thoughts that accompany the listening! Yes, we would not doubt that he enjoyed it if he could not name such experiences; but we would doubt it if it became apparent that he does not understand certain connections.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#35.2) <span class="fac">[35\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/35.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-352}

<span class="series-number">171</span> But doesn’t understanding also show itself, for example, in the way someone reads the poem, sings the melody? Certainly. But what is the experience during reading? One would have to say: the one who enjoys it and understands it, hears it read well, or feels it in the vocal organs.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#35.3) <span class="fac">[35\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/35.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-353}

<span class="series-number">172</span> One can also say that understanding a musical _phrase_ is the same as understanding a _language_.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#35.4) <span class="fac">[35\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/35.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-354}

<span class="series-number">173</span> I am thinking of a very short one, only two measures long. You say “What isn’t contained in it!” But it is only, so to speak, an optical illusion, if you think that what is in it is what happens when listening. (“It depends on _who_ says it.”) (Only in the stream of thoughts and of life do the words have meaning.)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#35.5) <span class="fac">[35\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/35.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-355}

<span class="series-number">174</span> Not _that_ contains the illusion: “_Now_ I understand it.” – and then perhaps a long explanation of what I understood follows.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#36.2) <span class="fac">[36\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/36.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-362}

<span class="series-number">175</span> Does the theme point to nothing other than itself? Oh yes! That is to say: – the impression it makes on me is connected with things in its surroundings – e.g. with our language and its intonation, that is, with the whole field of our language-games. If I say, for example: it is as if a conclusion is being drawn here, or as if something is being emphasized here, or as _if_ this were an answer to the earlier one, – then my understanding presupposes familiarity with conclusions, emphases, answers.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#36.3) <span class="fac">[36\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/36.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-363}

<span class="series-number">176</span> The words “Thank God! We have managed to escape something else – from the clutches of the Croats”, with their tone and context, seem already to contain every nuance of their meaning. But only because we know them as part of a particular scene. One could, however, construct a completely different scene around these words (spoken in the same tone); in order to show how their particular essence lies in the story to which they belong.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#36.4) <span class="fac">[36\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/36.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-364}

<span class="series-number">177</span> If I hear someone say “Move aside!” with an admonishing gesture, do I ‘experience’ here the meaning of the word in the same way as in a game, when I say it to myself & pronounce it sometimes _this_ way & sometimes _that_ way, and in doing so ‘mean’ it? – For he could also have said “Move away from me,” & then perhaps I would have experienced the whole phrase in this way or that; but also the single word? Perhaps it was the other words that made the impression on me.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#36.5) <span class="fac">[36\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/36.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-365}

<span class="series-number">178</span> The distinctive experience of meaning is characterized by the fact that we respond with an explanation & the past tense: just as if we were explaining the meaning of a word for practical purposes.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#36.6) <span class="fac">[36\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/36.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-366}

<span class="series-number">179</span> _Forget_, forget that you yourself had these experiences.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#37.1) <span class="fac">[37\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/37.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-371}

<span class="series-number">180</span> How could he hear the word in its meaning? How was it possible?! Not at all—in _these_ dimensions.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#37.2) <span class="fac">[37\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/37.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-372}

<span class="series-number">181</span> But isn’t it true that the word means that to me now? Why not? After all, this sense does not conflict with the other uses of the word. Someone says: “Give him the message… & _mean_ by that…!” – – What would be the sense of this command?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#37.3) <span class="fac">[37\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/37.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-373}

<span class="series-number">182</span> “When I uttered the word just now, it meant something to me…”. Why shouldn’t that simply be madness? Because _I_ experienced it? That is no reason.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#37.4) <span class="fac">[37\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/37.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-374}

<span class="series-number">183</span> The one I call “meaning-blind” will probably understand the instruction: “Tell him to go to the bank – I mean the garden bench,” but not: “Say the word ‘bank’ and mean the garden bench.” This investigation is not concerned with what forms of mental defects are found in people; rather, it is concerned with the possibility of such forms. Not whether there are people who are incapable of a thought of the type “I wanted to… at the time,” – but how the concept of such a defect could be carried out, is important.

If you assume that one person cannot do _that_, how is it then with _the other_? Shouldn’t he also be incapable of doing it? – Where does this concept lead us? Because we do, after all, have paradigms here.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#37.5) <span class="fac">[37\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/37.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-375}

<span class="series-number">184</span> Different people experience the change in the spelling of a word with vastly different intensity. And this sensation is not merely reverence for an old use. Someone for whom orthography is only a practical matter lacks a certain feeling, similar to the feeling that would be lacking in someone who is “blind to meaning.” (Goethe on personal names. The prisoner’s number.)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#37.6+38.1) <span class="fac">[37\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/37.webp),[38\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/38.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-376381}

<span class="series-number">185</span> Just as some people don’t understand the question “What color does the vowel _a_ have for you?” – if someone didn’t understand it, if they explained that it was nonsense, – could we say that they don’t understand German, or the meaning of the words “color,” “vowel,” etc.? On the contrary: if they have learned to understand these words, then they can also respond to those questions ‘with understanding’ or ‘without understanding’.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#38.2) <span class="fac">[38\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/38.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-382}

<span class="series-number">186</span> Misunderstanding – incomprehension. Understanding is brought about through explanation; but also through training.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#38.3) <span class="fac">[38\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/38.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-383}

<span class="series-number">187</span> Why can one not teach a cat to retrieve? Does it not understand what one wants? And what is the difference between understanding and lack of understanding here?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#38.4) <span class="fac">[38\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/38.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-384}

<span class="series-number">188</span> “I read every word with the feeling that corresponds to it. 1 But the word, for example, with the feeling of ‘but’ – and so on.” – And even if that is true, what does it actually mean? What is the grammar of the concept ‘feeling of but’? – The fact that I call it a “feeling” does not make it one.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#38.5) <span class="fac">[38\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/38.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-385}

<span class="series-number">189</span> Is lying a specific kind of experience? Well, can one say to someone, “I will now lie to you,” and then do it?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#38.6) <span class="fac">[38\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/38.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-386}

<span class="series-number">190</span> Intention. In what way am I conscious of the lie while I am lying? Only in so far as it does not only become conscious to me _later_, and I later know that I lied. Being conscious of the lie is a _capability_. This does not contradict the fact that there are characteristic feelings of lying. Intention.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#38.7) <span class="fac">[38\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/38.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-387}

<span class="series-number">191</span> Knowledge is not simply _translated_ into words when it is expressed. The words are not a translation of something else that was there before them.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#38.8) <span class="fac">[38\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/38.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-388}

<span class="series-number">192</span> “Misunderstanding something is a particular inner process.” – But what kind of process – even if you were to invent it – could accomplish what we demand of intention?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#38.9+39.1) <span class="fac">[38\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/38.webp),[39\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/39.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-389391}

<span class="series-number">193</span> Isn’t this exactly the same with the verb “to understand”? Someone explains to me the route I have to take to get there and there. He asks, “Have you understood me?” I answer, “I have understood.” – Do I want to communicate to him what was going on in me during his explanation? – And yet, that could also be communicated.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#39.2) <span class="fac">[39\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/39.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-392}

<span class="series-number">194</span> Imagine this game: A list of words from different languages and of senseless sequences of sounds is read to me. I am to say after each one whether I understand it or not; also, what was going on in me while understanding or not understanding. – I will answer “yes” to the word “tree” without thinking (an image may float before me); I will answer just as unhesitatingly with “no” to a sequence of sounds that I have never heard before. With words that designate a specific shade of color, a visualization of the answer will often precede it; with rare words (“continuum,” for example), there will be some deliberation; with words like the article “the,” perhaps a shrug of the shoulders; words from a foreign language I will _sometimes_ translate into German; if images float before me, they are sometimes those of the objects that are designated by the words (again, thousands of cases), sometimes other images. One could supplement this game with another, in which someone names _activities_ and asks with each one: “Can you do that?” – The subject should indicate what reasons he had for answering the question with “yes” or “no.”

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#39.3) <span class="fac">[39\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/39.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-393}

<span class="series-number">195</span> Let us imagine a kind of puzzle picture in which not _one_ particular object is to be found, but what appears to us at first glance as a jumble of meaningless lines, and after some searching is only then revealed as, say, a landscape picture. – In what consists the difference between the view of the picture before and after the solution? That we see it differently in both cases is clear. In what way, however, can one say that after the solution, the picture now tells us something, whereas before it told us nothing?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#39.4) <span class="fac">[39\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/39.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-394}

<span class="series-number">196</span> We can also put this question in this way: What is the general characteristic of having found the solution?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#40.1+41.1) <span class="fac">[40\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/40.webp),[41\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/41.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-401411}

<span class="series-number">197</span> I want to assume that as soon as it is solved, I make the solution recognizable by strongly emphasizing certain lines of the picture and perhaps adding shadows. Why do you now call the picture you have drawn a solution? a) Because it is the clear representation of a group of spatial objects. b) Because it is the representation of a regular body. c) Because it is a symmetrical figure. d) Because it is a figure that makes a decorative impression on me. e) Because it is the representation of a body that seems familiar to me. f) Because there is a list of solutions and this figure (this body) is on the list. g) Because it represents a kind of object that I probably know: for it makes on me the immediate impression of familiarity, I immediately connect all possible associations with it; I know what its name is; I know that I have often seen it; I know what it is used for: etc. h) Because I seem to know the object well: a word immediately occurs to me as its name (although the word does not belong to any existing language); I say to myself “Of course, it is a …” and give myself a nonsensical explanation that seems meaningful to me at this moment. (As in a dream.) i) Because it represents a face that is familiar to me. j) Because it represents a face that I recognize: it is the face of my friend N; it is a face that I have often seen depicted. etc. k) Because it represents an object that I remember seeing once. l) Because it is an ornament that I know well (although I don’t know where I saw it). m) Because it is an ornament that I know well: I know its name, I know where I have seen it before. n) Because it represents an item of furniture in my room. o) Because I instinctively emphasized these lines and now feel at ease. p) Because I remember that this object has been described to me. And so on. (Whoever does not understand why we are talking about these things must regard what we say as empty play.)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#41.2) <span class="fac">[41\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/41.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-412}

<span class="series-number">198</span> Can I imagine away the impression of familiarity where it is present, and imagine it in where it is not? And what does that mean? I look, for example, at the face of a friend and ask myself: What does this face look like if I see it as a face that is unfamiliar to me (as if I were seeing it for the first time)? What, in a way, remains of the sight of the face if I imagine away, subtract, the impression of familiarity? – Here I am inclined to say: “It is _very difficult_ to separate the familiarity from the impression of the face.” But I also feel that this is a bad way of putting it. For I don’t even know how to try to separate these two. The expression “to separate them” has no clear sense for me. I know what it _means_: “Imagine this table black instead of brown.” This corresponds to: “Paint these tables, but black instead of brown.”

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#41.3) <span class="fac">[41\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/41.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-413}

<span class="series-number">199</span> What if one said: “Imagine this butterfly, exactly as it is, but ugly instead of beautiful”?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#41.4) <span class="fac">[41\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/41.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-414}

<span class="series-number">200</span> _We_ have not _defined_ what it means to imagine away the familiarity. It could mean, for example, to remember the impression I had when I saw the face for the first time.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#41.5+42.1) <span class="fac">[41\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/41.webp),[42\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/42.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-415421}

<span class="series-number">201</span> The pictorial representation of the inside of a radio receiver will be a jumble of senseless lines for someone who is unfamiliar with such things. But if he has become acquainted with the device and its function, then that drawing will be a meaningful picture for him. Now, given any physical shape that is currently senseless to me (for example, in a picture) – can I imagine it in a meaningful way at will? That would be like asking: Can I imagine any arbitrarily shaped object as an object for use? But for what use? One could methodically think of a class of body shapes as dwellings for animals or humans. Another class as weapons. Another as models of landscapes. Etc. etc. And here I know how I can imbue a senseless form with meaning.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#42.2) <span class="fac">[42\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/42.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-422}

<span class="series-number">202</span> Consider how we use the word “recognize”! I recognize the furniture in my room, my friend, whom I see every day. But no ‘re-recognition’ takes place.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#42.3) <span class="fac">[42\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/42.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-423}

<span class="series-number">203</span> One could say: I would have no impression of the room as a whole if I could not quickly let my gaze wander around in it and move freely in it. (Stream of thought.) But now the question is how does it manifest that I have an impression of it as a whole? For example: in the naturalness with which I find my way in it; in the absence of searching, doubting and wondering; in the fact that I find it useful & necessary to repeatedly use the concept ‘my room’, as opposed to its walls, corners, etc.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#42.4) <span class="fac">[42\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/42.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-424}

<span class="series-number">204</span> What does the description of an ‘attitude’ look like? One says, for example: “Look away from these spots and also from this small irregularity, and look at it as a picture of…” “Imagine that away! Would it also be unpleasant to you without this…?” One would say that I am changing my visual image – as if by blinking, or by keeping out a detail. This “looking away from…” plays a very similar role to, for example, creating a new picture.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#42.5+43.1) <span class="fac">[42\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/42.webp),[43\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/43.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-425431}

<span class="series-number">205</span> Now then, – and these are good reasons for saying that we have changed our visual impression through our attitude. That is, these are good reasons for limiting the concept of ‘visual impression’ in this way.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#43.2) <span class="fac">[43\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/43.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-432}

<span class="series-number">206</span> “But I can obviously bring elements (lines, for example) together in _seeing_!” But why does one call it “bringing together”? Why is there a need for a word – _essential_ – that already has a different meaning? (It is, of course, as in the case of the word “mental **calculation**”.)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#43.3) <span class="fac">[43\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/43.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-433}

<span class="series-number">207</span> If I say to someone: “Combine these lines (or something else)!” what will he do? Well, different things, depending on the circumstances. Perhaps he should count them two and two, or put them in a drawer, or look at them, etc.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#43.4+44.1) <span class="fac">[43\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/43.webp),[44\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/44.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-434441}

<span class="series-number">208</span> It is useful to consider what one says about a phenomenon, such as this one:

The figure

![](../graphics/233a,43.svg)

one can see it sometimes as an F, sometimes as its mirror image of an F. Now I want to ask: In what does it consist, to see the figure sometimes in one way, sometimes in another? – Do I really see something different each time? or do I only _interpret_ what I see in different ways? – I am inclined to say the former. _But why?_ Well, interpreting is an action. It can, for example, consist in someone saying “This is meant to be an F,” or not saying it, but replacing the sign with an F when copying; or considering: “What could this be? It will be an F that the writer made badly.” – Seeing is not an action, but a state (grammatical remark). And if I have never taken it to be anything other than an F, never considered what it might be, then one will say that I _see_ it as an F, namely, when one knows that it can also be seen differently. I would call it “interpreting” if I said: “This is certainly an ‘F’; the writer writes all his ‘F’s like this.” How did one come to the concept of seeing this as that in the first place? In what situations did he have a need for it? (Very common in art.) There, for example, where it is a matter of phrasing through the eye or ear. We say “You have to hear these bars as an introduction,” “You have to listen for this key,” “If you have seen this figure once as..., it is difficult to see it differently,” “I hear the French ‘ne… pas’ as a two-part negation, but not as ‘not a step’”, etc. etc. Is it now a real seeing or hearing? Well: that is what we call it; with these words we react in certain situations. And we react to these words again with certain actions.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#44.2) <span class="fac">[44\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/44.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-442}

<span class="series-number">209</span> This shape that I see – I would like to say – is not simply _one_ shape, but it is one of the shapes I am familiar with; it is a shape that has been distinguished in advance. It is one of the shapes whose picture was already in me earlier, and only because it corresponds to such a picture, is it the well-known shape. (I carry, as it were, a catalog of such shapes with me, and the objects depicted there are then the well-known ones. –

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#44.3) <span class="fac">[44\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/44.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-443}

<span class="series-number">210</span> But the fact that I carried the picture with me earlier would only be a causal explanation of the present impression. It is as if one said: this movement goes so easily as if it had been practiced.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#44.4) <span class="fac">[44\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/44.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-444}

<span class="series-number">211</span> “If I am asked ‘Do you see a sphere there?’ and another time ‘Do you see the hemisphere there?’, then what I see can be the same both times, and if I answer ‘Yes,’ then I still distinguish between the two hypotheses. As in chess, I distinguish between a pawn and the king, even if the present move is one that both could make, and even if a king figure were functioning as a pawn.” – One is always in danger in philosophy of creating a myth of symbolism, or one of the mental processes. Instead of simply saying what everyone knows and must admit.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#44.5) <span class="fac">[44\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/44.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-445}

<span class="series-number">212</span> Is it introspection that teaches me whether I am dealing with real seeing or with interpreting? First of all, I must become clear about what I would call interpreting; what shows that something is to be called interpreting or seeing. Interpreting corresponds to seeing. The temptation is again great to say, my [unreadable]

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#45.1) <span class="fac">[45\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/45.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-451}

<span class="series-number">213</span> Do I not see the figure sometimes in one way, sometimes in another, even if I do not react with words? But “sometimes in one way,” “sometimes in another” are, after all, words, and on what grounds may I use them here? Can I prove it, or prove it to myself? (Unless by means of a different reaction.) But I do know that these are two different impressions, even if I don’t say so! But how do I know that what I then say is what I knew? What consequences follow from the fact that I interpret this as that? What consequences follow from the fact that I see this as that?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#45.2) <span class="fac">[45\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/45.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-452}

<span class="series-number">214</span> Experience of real size. We see a picture that shows the shape of an armchair; we are told that it represents a structure the size of a house. Now we see it differently.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#45.3) <span class="fac">[45\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/45.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-453}

<span class="series-number">215</span> Imagine someone who, while looking at the sun, suddenly has the _sensation_ that it is not moving, but rather that we are passing by it. Now he wants to say that he has seen a new state of motion in which we find ourselves; and suppose he now shows, through gestures, what motion he means, and that it is not the motion of the sun. – We would be dealing with two different applications of the word “motion.”

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#45.4) <span class="fac">[45\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/45.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-454}

<span class="series-number">216</span> One does not see the change of aspect, but the change of interpretation.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#45.5) <span class="fac">[45\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/45.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-455}

<span class="series-number">217</span> You do not see it according to one interpretation, but according to an interpreting.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#45.7) <span class="fac">[45\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/45.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-457}

<span class="series-number">218</span> I interpret the words; yes, perhaps – but do I also interpret facial expressions? Do I interpret a facial expression as threatening or friendly? It _can_ happen. If I were to say, “It is not enough that I perceive the threatening face, but I must first interpret it,” – someone points a knife at me, and I say, “I take this as a threat.”

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#45.8) <span class="fac">[45\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/45.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-458}

<span class="series-number">219</span> We understand Chinese gestures as little as we understand Chinese sentences.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#46.1) <span class="fac">[46\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/46.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-461}

<span class="series-number">220</span> Consciousness in the face of the other. Look into the face of the other, & see the consciousness in him & a particular **tone** of consciousness. You see in him, on him, joy, indifference, interest, emotion, dullness, etc. The light in the face of the other. Do you look into _yourself_ to recognize the anger in _his_ face? It is there as clearly as in your own breast.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#46.2) <span class="fac">[46\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/46.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-462}

(And what is one to say now? That the face of the other stimulates me to imitate, and that I therefore feel small movements and muscle tensions in myself, and that this is the sum of _mine_? Nonsense. Nonsense – because you make assumptions instead of simply describing. Whoever has explanations spinning around in his head neglects to reflect on the most important facts.)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#46.3) <span class="fac">[46\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/46.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-463}

<span class="series-number">221</span> “Consciousness is as clearly present in his face and behaviour as it is in me myself.”

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#46.4) <span class="fac">[46\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/46.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-464}

<span class="series-number">222</span> We do not see the human eye as a receiver; it does not seem to let something in, but rather to send something out. The ear receives; the eye looks. (It throws glances, it flashes, it shines.) One can scare someone with the eye, not with the ear or nose. When you see the eye, you see something emanating from it. You see the gaze of the eye.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#46.5+47.1) <span class="fac">[46\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/46.webp),[47\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/47.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-465471}

<span class="series-number">223</span> “If you would only abandon your physiological prejudices, you would find nothing at all objectionable in the idea that the act of looking with the eye can also be seen.” I also say that I see the look that you give to another person. And if one were to correct me and say that I do not actually _see_ it, I would consider that mere foolishness. On the other hand, with my way of speaking, I have not _conceded_ anything, and I contradict the person who tells me that I see the look ‘in the same way’ as the shape and color of the eye. For ‘naive speech,’ i.e., our naive, normal way of expressing ourselves, contains no theory of seeing – it does not show you a theory, but only a concept of seeing.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#47.2) <span class="fac">[47\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/47.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-472}

<span class="series-number">224</span> Let a person look angry, haughty, ironic; and now let his face be covered so that only the eyes remain free – in which the whole expression seemed to be united: its expression is now surprisingly _ambiguous_.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#47.3) <span class="fac">[47\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/47.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-473}

<span class="series-number">225</span> “One _sees_ a mood.” – In contrast to what? – One does not see the facial expressions and _infers_ (as the doctor, who makes a diagnosis) joy, sadness, boredom. One describes his face directly as sad, joyful, bored, even if one is not able to give another description of the facial features. – One might say that the sadness is personified in the face. This belongs to the concept of a mood.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#47.4) <span class="fac">[47\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/47.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-474}

<span class="series-number">226</span> (The ugliness of a person can be repulsive in a picture, in a painting, as in reality, but also in a description, in words.)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#47.5) <span class="fac">[47\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/47.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-475}

<span class="series-number">227</span> It is strange: we would like to explain our understanding of a gesture by translating it into words, and the understanding of words by translating them into gestures. (Thus, we are tossed back and forth when we want to find out where the understanding actually lies.) And indeed, we explain words by a gesture, and a gesture by words.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#47.6) <span class="fac">[47\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/47.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-476}

<span class="series-number">228</span> Explain to someone that the pointer setting, which you have recorded, is meant to express: the pointers of this clock are now set this way. – – The awkwardness with which the sign, like a mute person, tries to make itself understood through all sorts of suggestive gestures – it disappears when we recognize that it depends on the _system_ to which the sign belongs. One would like to say: only the _thought_ can _say_ it, not the sign.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#47.7+48.1) <span class="fac">[47\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/47.webp),[48\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/48.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-477481}

<span class="series-number">229</span> An _interpretation_ is something that is given in a sign. It is this interpretation in contrast to another (which sounds different). – If one wanted to say “Every sentence requires a further interpretation,” then that would mean: no sentence can be understood without an addition.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#48.2) <span class="fac">[48\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/48.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-482}

<span class="series-number">230</span> It would be almost the same as if, when rolling dice, one determined how much a roll should count by means of a further roll.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#48.3) <span class="fac">[48\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/48.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-483}

<span class="series-number">231</span> By “intention,” I mean here what the sign uses in thought. The intention seems to interpret, to give the definitive interpretation; but not another sign or picture, but something else, something that one cannot interpret again. But a psychological end is reached, not a logical one. Let us think of a sign language, an ‘abstract’ one, I mean one that is foreign to us, in which we do not feel at home, in which, as we would say, we do not _think_; and let us imagine this language interpreted by a translation into a, as we would like to say, unambiguous picture language; a language that consists of pictures painted in perspective. It is quite clear that it is much easier to think of different _interpretations_ of the written characters than of a picture painted in a familiar way. Here, we will also be inclined to think that there is no longer any possibility of interpretation.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#48.4) <span class="fac">[48\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/48.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-484}

<span class="series-number">232</span> We could also say that we do not live in the sign language, but rather in the painted picture.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#48.5+49.1) <span class="fac">[48\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/48.webp),[49\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/49.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-485491}

<span class="series-number">233</span> “Only the intended picture suffices as a standard in relation to reality. When viewed from the outside, it stands there, dead and isolated.” – It is as if we first looked at a picture in such a way that we live in it and the objects in it surround us as real, and then we step back and are now outside, see the frame, and the picture is a painted surface. So, when we intend, the _pictures_ of the intention surround us and we live among them. But when we step out of the intention, they are merely spots on a canvas, without life and without interest for us. When we intend, we live in the space of the intention, among the pictures (shadows) of the intention, together with the real things. Let us think, we are sitting in the darkened cinema and living in the film. The hall is now illuminated, but the play of light on the screen continues. But now we suddenly stand outside, and see it as movements of light and dark spots on a canvas. (In a dream, it sometimes happens that we first read a story and then act in it ourselves. And after waking up from a dream, it is sometimes as if we have stepped out of the dream and now see it as a foreign picture in front of us.) And it also means something to “live in the pages of a book”.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#49.2) <span class="fac">[49\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/49.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-492}

<span class="series-number">234</span> It is not the case that this symbol can no longer be interpreted, but rather: I do not interpret. I do not interpret because I feel at _home_ in the present picture. If I interpret, I proceed on my path of thought from stage to stage.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#49.3) <span class="fac">[49\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/49.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-493}

<span class="series-number">235</span> If I look at the imagined symbol “from the outside,” it becomes conscious to me that it _could_ be interpreted in this way or that; if it is a stage in my train of thought, then it is a place that is natural to me, & its further interpretability does not occupy (and disturb) me. – As is the case when I have the chart, the railway timetable, without it occupying me that a chart can be interpreted in different ways.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#49.4+50.1) <span class="fac">[49\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/49.webp),[50\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/50.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-494501}

<span class="series-number">236</span> If I want to describe the process of intention, I feel above all that it can best achieve what it should when it contains a very faithful picture of what it intends. But further, that even that is not enough, because the picture, whatever it is, can be interpreted in different ways; so that this picture again stands isolated. As one considers the picture alone, it is suddenly dead, and it is as if something has been taken away from it that had enlivened it before. It is no thought, no intention, and however we think of it as accompanied by articulated or unarticulated processes, and by whatever sensations: it remains isolated, does not point from itself to a reality outside of it. Now one says: “Of course, the picture does not intend, but we must intend _something_ with it.” But if this intending, meaning, is again something that _happens with the picture_, then I do not see why it should be bound to a human being. One can also study the process of digestion as a chemical process, independently of whether it takes place in a living being. We want to say “Meaning is essentially a mental process, a process of conscious life, not of dead matter.” But what is meant by such a process, other than the specific kind of what is happening – as long as we are thinking of a process. And now it seems to us as if no process, of whatever kind, can be the intending. – We are simply not satisfied with the grammar of the _process_, and not with the specific kind of _a_ process. – One could say: we would call every process “dead” in this sense!

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#50.2) <span class="fac">[50\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/50.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-502}

<span class="series-number">237</span> One could almost say: “The opinion _goes_, while every _process_ stands”.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#50.3) <span class="fac">[50\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/50.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-503}

<span class="series-number">238</span> One says: How can this gesture, this posture of the hand, this picture, be the wish that this and that were the case? It is nothing more than a hand over a table, and it stands alone and without _sense_! Like a single stage set that has been left standing in a room after a theatrical performance. It only had meaning within the play.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#50.4) <span class="fac">[50\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/50.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-504}

<span class="series-number">239</span> “The thought stood before my mind at this moment.” – And how? – “I had this image.” – So was the image the thought? No, because if I had only communicated the image to someone, he would not have grasped the thought.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#50.5) <span class="fac">[50\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/50.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-505}

<span class="series-number">240</span> The image was the key. Or it _appeared_ as the key.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#51.1) <span class="fac">[51\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/51.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-511}

<span class="series-number">241</span> Let us imagine a sequence of pictures, in schematic form, so that it is more like a narrative in a language than a sequence of realistic pictures. One could, in such a picture language, have captured something, in particular the course of battles. (Language-game.) And a sentence of our word-language is closer to such a picture in this picture language than one might think.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#51.2) <span class="fac">[51\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/51.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-512}

<span class="series-number">242</span> Let us also remember that we do not first have to translate such images into realistic ones in order to ‘understand’ them, just as we never translate photographs or the images of a film into colored images, even though black and white people, or plants in reality, seem incredibly strange and terrible to us. What if we now said: “An image is something only in a picture language”?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#51.3) <span class="fac">[51\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/51.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-513}

<span class="series-number">243</span> Of course, I read a story and don't give a damn about a system of language. I simply read, have impressions, see images before me, etc. I let the story pass before me like images, like a picture story. (Of course, I don't mean to say that every sentence evokes a visual image, or several, in me, and that that is perhaps the purpose of a sentence.)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#51.4) <span class="fac">[51\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/51.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-514}

<span class="series-number">244</span> “Sentences are meant to describe how everything is related,” we think. The sentence as _image_.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#51.5) <span class="fac">[51\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/51.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-515}

<span class="series-number">245</span> I understand this image exactly; I could represent it plastically. – I understand this description exactly; I could make a drawing according to it. In many cases, one could establish as a criterion of understanding that one must be able to represent the sense of the sentence graphically. (I am thinking of an officially established test of understanding.) How, for example, is one tested in card reading?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#52.1) <span class="fac">[52\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/52.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-521}

<span class="series-number">246</span> And the meaningful image is the one that I can not only draw but also represent plastically. And to say this would make sense. But the thinking of the sentence is not an activity that one performs according to the words (as one might sing according to the notes). The following example shows this. Does it make sense to say: “I have as many friends as there are solutions to the equation …”? Whether this makes sense is not immediately apparent from the equation. And one could, in this sense, not know whether the sentence can be thought or not. Whether it can be understood or not.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#52.2) <span class="fac">[52\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/52.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-522}

<span class="series-number">247</span> What does it mean to “discover that a sentence has no sense”? And what does it mean: “if I mean something by it, it must have sense”? The first means: not to be misled by the appearance of a sentence and to examine its use in the language-game. And “if I mean something by it” – does that mean something _similar_ to: “if I can imagine something with it”? – From the imagination, there is a path to further use.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#52.3) <span class="fac">[52\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/52.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-523}

<span class="series-number">248</span> (Something that looks like a sentence at first glance, but isn't one.) The following proposal for the construction of a road roller was once communicated to me. The motor is located inside the hollow roller. The crankshaft runs through the middle of the roller and is connected to the roller rim at both ends by spokes. The cylinder of the motor is attached to the inside of the roller. At first glance, this construction looks like a machine. But it is a rigid system, and the piston cannot move in and out of the cylinder. We have deprived it of the possibility of movement and do not know it.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#52.4+53.1) <span class="fac">[52\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/52.webp),[53\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/53.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-524531}

<span class="series-number">249</span> “Nothing is easier than imagining a 4-dimensional cube! It looks like this:

– But that is not what I mean; I mean something like

just with 4 dimensions! – “But isn’t what I showed you something like

just with 4 dimensions?” – No; that is not what I _mean_! – – But what do I mean? What is my _picture_? Well, the 4-dimensional cube, as you have drawn it, is not it! I now have only the _words_ as my image, and the rejection of everything that you can show me.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#53.2) <span class="fac">[53\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/53.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-532}

<span class="series-number">250</span> Are roses red in the dark? – One can think of the rose in the dark as red. – The fact that one can ‘think’ of something does not mean that it makes sense to say it.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#53.3) <span class="fac">[53\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/53.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-533}

<span class="series-number">251</span> “The assumption that this person – who behaves quite normally – is nevertheless blind, does make sense!” – That is to say: ‘it is indeed an assumption’, ‘I can indeed make such an assumption’. And that means: I form an image of what I assume. Yes; but does it go further? If I make the assumption that someone is blind, under other circumstances, I never confirm that this assumption really makes sense. And the fact that I really think something, have an image, then plays no role at all. This image only becomes important here, where it is, as it were, the only indication that I have actually made an assumption. Yes, it is all that is left of an assumption here.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#53.4) <span class="fac">[53\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/53.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-534}

<span class="series-number">252</span> “I can very well imagine that someone acts in such a way and yet sees nothing shameful in the action” – and now follows a description of how one should imagine it. “I can imagine a human society in which it is considered indecent to calculate, except for pastime.” This means approximately: I could easily paint this image further.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#53.5) <span class="fac">[53\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/53.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-535}

<span class="series-number">253</span> “I have never actually seen a black spot gradually become lighter until it is white, and then the white becoming redder until it is red. But I know that it is possible because I can imagine it.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#53.6) <span class="fac">[53\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/53.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-536}

<span class="series-number">254</span> 74 (When one is talking to someone about a time division, it often happens that one pulls out the clock, not to see what time it is, but to be able to form an image of the division being thought about.)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#54.1) <span class="fac">[54\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/54.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-541}

<span class="series-number">255</span> How can one learn the truth through thinking?

How one learns to see a face better by drawing it.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#54.2) <span class="fac">[54\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/54.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-542}

<span class="series-number">256</span> The philosophers who believe that one can expand experience in thinking should remember that one can transmit speech through the telephone, but not measles. I cannot also feel time as limited, if I will, or the field of vision as homogeneous, etc.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#54.3) <span class="fac">[54\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/54.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-543}

<span class="series-number">257</span> Would it be possible to discover a _new_ color? (Because a colorblind person is in the same situation as we are; their colors form just as complete a system as ours; they see no gap where the other colors still belong.) (Comparison with mathematics.)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#54.4) <span class="fac">[54\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/54.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-544}

<span class="series-number">258</span> In logic, one cannot extend generality any further than our logical foresight allows. Or rather: than our logical vision allows.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#54.5) <span class="fac">[54\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/54.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-545}

<span class="series-number">259</span> “But how can the human mind outstrip reality, & even _think_ about what is unverifiable?” – Why shouldn’t we _talk_ about the unverifiable? After all, we ourselves made it unverifiable. Does it create a false _appearance_? & how can it even seem that way? Do you want to say that this _state_ isn’t even a description? Well, then it isn’t a _false_ appearance, but rather one that deprives us of our bearings, so that we are left to ask: How is it possible?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#54.6) <span class="fac">[54\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/54.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-546}

<span class="series-number">260</span> One can only seemingly “go beyond every possible experience”: yes, _this word_ also only seems to have sense, because it is formed by analogy with more meaningful expressions.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#54.7) <span class="fac">[54\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/54.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-547}

<span class="series-number">261</span> The “philosophy of ‘as if’” is based _itself_ on this confusion between simile & reality.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#54.8) <span class="fac">[54\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/54.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-548}

<span class="series-number">262</span> “One cannot sneak into a thought, through words, a premonition of something that one _does not know_. (There is nothing in the intellect…) As if one could, in thought, come at it from the back, and catch a glimpse of something that is impossible to see from the front.”

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#54.9) <span class="fac">[54\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/54.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-549}

<span class="series-number">263</span> Therefore, there is some truth to the idea that imaginability is a criterion of nonsensicality.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#54.10) <span class="fac">[54\[10\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/54.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-5410}

<span class="series-number">264</span> How, if one were to say: “I cannot imagine what it is like to see an armchair, except when I am actually _seeing_ it”? Would it be justified to say that?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#54.11) <span class="fac">[54\[11\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/54.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-5411}

<span class="series-number">265</span> _Am I justified_ in saying “I cannot see it as a shape”? What justifies me in doing so? (What justifies the blind person in saying that he cannot see?)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#55.1) <span class="fac">[55\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/55.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-551}

<span class="series-number">266</span> Can one imagine absolute pitch if one does not possess it? – Can one imagine it, _if_ one does possess it? – Can a blind person imagine seeing? Can _I_ imagine it? – Can I imagine that I react in such and such a way spontaneously, if I do not do so? – Can I imagine it better if I do? (This relates to the question: Can I imagine that someone sees !!!!!!!! as an articulated shape?)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#55.2) <span class="fac">[55\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/55.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-552}

<span class="series-number">267</span> Is it to be an empirical fact that whoever has had an experience can imagine it, and that another _cannot_? (How do I know that the blind man can imagine colors?) But: he cannot play a language-game (cannot learn it). But how? According to experience, or eo ipso? The latter.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#55.3) <span class="fac">[55\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/55.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-553}

<span class="series-number">268</span> What would we say to someone who claimed that they could accurately imagine what it is like to have perfect pitch, without actually having it?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#55.4) <span class="fac">[55\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/55.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-554}

<span class="series-number">269</span> If one believes one can imagine a four-dimensional space, why not also four-dimensional colors—that is, colors that, in addition to the degree of saturation, the hue, and the luminosity, allow for a fourth determination?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#55.5) <span class="fac">[55\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/55.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-555}

<span class="series-number">270</span> “How can it possibly make sense to talk about a completely new kind of sensory perception that one might perhaps experience someday? Unless you are talking about the sensory **organ**.”

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#55.6) <span class="fac">[55\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/55.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-556}

<span class="series-number">271</span> What purpose does a sentence like this serve: “We cannot possibly imagine the sensations of a juggler like Rastelli”?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#55.8) <span class="fac">[55\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/55.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-558}

<span class="series-number">272</span> “It makes sense to talk about an endless row of trees; I can imagine that a row of trees continues endlessly.” That is, if it makes sense to say that the row of trees ends here, then it makes sense to say that it does not end here, and therefore also that it does not end anywhere.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#56.2) <span class="fac">[56\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/56.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-562}

<span class="series-number">273</span> Hardy: “That ‘the finite cannot understand the infinite’ should surely be a theological and not a mathematical war cry.” It is true, this expression is awkward. But what people mean to say by it is: “Surely, things must be done properly here! Where does this leap from the finite to the infinite come from?” And the way of expressing it is not entirely nonsensical – only, the ‘finite,’ which the infinite should not be able to think, is not ‘man,’ or ‘our understanding,’ but the calculus. And _**how**_ this thinks of the ‘infinite’ is probably worth investigating. And this is to be compared with the thorough investigation and clarification of a company’s business operations by a Chartered Accountant. The goal is a clear, comparative representation of all applications, illustrations, and conceptions of the calculus. A complete overview of everything that could create uncertainty. And this overview must extend over a wide area, because the roots of our ideas reach far. – “The finite cannot understand the infinite” means here: _So_ it cannot happen, as you, in characteristic superficiality, represent it. Thought can, as it were, _fly_; it does not need to walk. You do not understand, i.e., you overlook, your transactions, and you project, as it were, your lack of understanding into the idea of a medium in which the most astonishing things are possible.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#57.1) <span class="fac">[57\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/57.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-571}

<span class="series-number">274</span> The ‘truly infinite’ is a ‘mere word.’ It would be better to say: this expression provisionally only creates a picture – which is still floating in the air; its application you still owe us.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#57.2) <span class="fac">[57\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/57.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-572}

<span class="series-number">275</span> An infinitely long row of spheres, an infinitely long rod. Imagine that this is spoken of in a kind of fairy tale. What application, even if only fictitious, could one make of this concept? The question is now not: Can such a thing exist? But: What do we imagine? So let your imagination really run wild! You can have it however you want now. You only need to _say_ how you want it. So just make a word picture; illustrate it however you want – with drawings, with comparisons, etc.! You can, as it were, create a technical drawing. And now the question is, how is one to work according to it.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#57.3) <span class="fac">[57\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/57.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-573}

<span class="series-number">276</span> I think I see a drawing in the sequence, which only needs “etc.” to extend into infinity. “I see a characteristic in it.” – Well, something that corresponds to the algebraic expression. – “Yes, but nothing written, but rather something ethereal.” – What a strange picture. – “Something that is not the algebraic expression, but of which this is only the _expression_.”

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#57.4) <span class="fac">[57\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/57.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-574}

<span class="series-number">277</span> I see something in it – similar to a shape in an ambiguous picture. And if I see that, then I say “That is all I need.” – Whoever finds the signpost now no longer looks for further instructions, but he _goes_. (And if I said “he orients himself to it” instead of “he goes,” the difference between the two might only be that the second expression refers to certain psychological accompanying phenomena.)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#57.5) <span class="fac">[57\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/57.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-575}

<span class="series-number">278</span> What does it mean: One can extend a straight line as far as one likes! Is there not a “and so on ad infinitum” here that is quite different from that of mathematical induction? According to what has been said so far, the expression for the possibility of extending it would consist in the description of the extended piece, or of the extending. Here it seems, at first, to have nothing to do with numbers. I can imagine that the pencil, which draws the line, continues its movement and then always continues in the same way. But is it also conceivable that the possibility does not consist in being able to accompany this process with a countable process! I don’t think so.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#58.1) <span class="fac">[58\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/58.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-581}

<span class="series-number">279</span> When do we say: “The line gives me _the rule_ – always the same.” And on the other hand: “It keeps giving me what I have to do – it is not a rule.” In the first case, it means: I have no further instance for what I have to do. The rule does it all alone; I only have to follow it (and following it is just _one_ thing). I don’t, for example, feel: it is odd that the line will tell me what I have to do; the line will tell it to me.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#58.2) <span class="fac">[58\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/58.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-582}

<span class="series-number">280</span> One could imagine that someone, with such feelings, multiplies correctly, multiplies properly; always says “I don’t know – now the rule suddenly gives me _that_!” – and that we reply: “Of course; you are, after all, following the rule exactly.”

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#58.3) <span class="fac">[58\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/58.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-583}

<span class="series-number">281</span> To say that the points that this experiment yields lie on average on this line, e.g., a straight line, says something similar to: “Seen from this distance, they seem to lie on a straight line.” I can say of a line that the general impression is that of a straight line; but not of the line

![](../graphics/233a,58.svg)<span class="post-block-punct">;</span>

although it would be possible to see it as a piece of a longer line, in which the deviations from the straight line would be lost. I cannot say: “This line segment looks straight, because it can be a piece of a line that gives me the impression of a straight line as a whole.” (Mountains on the Earth and on the Moon: the Earth is a sphere.)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#58.4) <span class="fac">[58\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/58.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-584}

<span class="series-number">282</span> “It gives me, without responsibility, this or that” means: I cannot teach you _how_ I follow the line. I do not assume that you will follow it as I do, even if you follow it.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#58.5) <span class="fac">[58\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/58.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-585}

<span class="series-number">283</span> What does it mean: to understand that something is an order, when one does not yet understand the order itself? (“He means: I am supposed to do something – but _what_ he wants, I don’t know.”)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#58.6) <span class="fac">[58\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/58.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-586}

<span class="series-number">284</span> The sentence “I must understand the order before I can act according to it” naturally has a good sense; but again, no metalogical one.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#58.7) <span class="fac">[58\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/58.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-587}

<span class="series-number">285</span> The idea one has of understanding in this case is something like: it brings one closer to the execution, closer to the words. – In what sense is that correct?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#58.8) <span class="fac">[58\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/58.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-588}

<span class="series-number">286</span> “But I must understand an order in order to act according to it.” Here the “_must_” is suspicious. – Also think of the question: “How long before obeying must you understand the order?”

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#59.1) <span class="fac">[59\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/59.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-591}

<span class="series-number">287</span> “I cannot execute the command because I do not understand what you mean. ‒ ‒ Yes, now I understand you.” – What was happening when I suddenly understood the other person? There were many possibilities. The command, for example, could have been given with incorrect emphasis; and suddenly the correct emphasis occurred to me. I would then say to a third person, “Now I understand him, he means…” and repeat the command with the correct emphasis. And in the correct emphasis, I would now understand it; that is, I would no longer have to grasp a _sense_ (something _outside_ the sentence, i.e., something ethereal), but the well-known German wording would be completely sufficient for me. – Or, the command was given to me in understandable German, but it seemed nonsensical. Then an explanation occurs to me; and now I can execute it. – Or, several interpretations could have occurred to me, and I finally decide on one.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#59.2) <span class="fac">[59\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/59.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-592}

<span class="series-number">288</span> If the command is _not_ followed – where is the shadow of its execution, which you meant to see; because the form was floating before you: He commands _this and that._

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#59.3) <span class="fac">[59\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/59.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-593}

<span class="series-number">289</span> If the connection of meaning could have been established _before_ the command, then it could also be established after the command.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#59.4) <span class="fac">[59\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/59.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-594}

<span class="series-number">290</span> “He did _that_ which I commanded him to do.” – Why should one not say here: it is an identity of the action and the _**words**_?! Why should I put a shadow between the two? We have a projection method. – It is just a different identity: “I did what he did” and, on the other hand, “I did what he commanded.”

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#59.5) <span class="fac">[59\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/59.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-595}

<span class="series-number">291</span> “Connection of the image with what is depicted” could be called the rays of projection; but also the technique of projecting.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#59.6+60.1) <span class="fac">[59\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/59.webp),[60\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/60.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-596601}

<span class="series-number">292</span> The ambiguity of our way of speaking: If a command were given to us in a cipher and the key to translating it into German, then we could describe the process of forming the German command with the words: “derive from the cipher what we have to do,” or “derive what the execution of the command is.” If, on the other hand, we act according to the command, we follow it, then in certain cases one can also speak of deriving the execution.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#60.2) <span class="fac">[60\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/60.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-602}

<span class="series-number">293</span> I give the rules of a game. The other person, in complete accordance with these rules, makes a move, the possibility of which I did not foresee, and which disturbs the game as I intended. I must now say: “I gave bad rules”; I must change my rules, or perhaps supplement them. So, did I already have an image of the game in advance? In a certain sense: yes! It was, for example, possible that I did not foresee that a quadratic equation does not have to have real solutions. The rule thus leads me to something, of which I say: “this image I did not expect; I always imagined a solution _like this_: …”

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#60.3) <span class="fac">[60\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/60.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-603}

<span class="series-number">294</span> In one case, we make a move in an existing game, in the other, we set a rule of the game. One could also consider the making of a move with a game piece in these two _ways_: as a paradigm for future moves, and as a move of the game.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#60.4) <span class="fac">[60\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/60.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-604}

<span class="series-number">295</span> You must consider that there may be a language-game, ‘continuing a series of numbers’, in which no rule, no rule expression is ever given, but learning takes place _only_ through examples. So that the idea of a justification by an image that forces us to proceed in this way would be completely foreign to these people.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#60.5) <span class="fac">[60\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/60.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-605}
