<span class="series-number">296</span> How strange: It seems as if a physical (mechanical) guidance could fail, allow the unforeseen, but a rule cannot! It would be, as it were, the only reliable guidance. But what is it that a guidance does not allow a movement, and what is it that a rule does not allow it? – How does one know the one, and how the other?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#61.1) <span class="fac">[61\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/61.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-611}

<span class="series-number">297</span> How do I manage to use a word correctly every time, that is, meaningfully? Do I always consult the grammar? No; the fact that I have something in mind – what I have in mind – prevents me from saying nonsense.” – “I have something in mind with the words” means here: I _know_ that I can apply them. But I may believe that I can apply them, and it turns out that I was mistaken.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#61.2) <span class="fac">[61\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/61.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-612}

<span class="series-number">298</span> It does not follow that understanding is the activity through which we demonstrate our understanding. The question of whether it is this activity is misleading. It must not be understood _in this way_: “Is understanding _this_ activity – or is it not rather a _different_ one?” – But rather: “Is ‘understanding’ used to designate this activity – or is it used _differently_?”

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#61.3) <span class="fac">[61\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/61.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-613}

<span class="series-number">299</span> We say: “If you really follow the rule when multiplying, _the same_ result must come out.” Well, if this is only the somewhat hysterical expression of university language, then it doesn’t need to interest us very much. But it is the expression of an attitude towards the technique of calculation, which is evident everywhere in our lives. The emphasis on ‘must’ corresponds only to the inflexibility of this attitude, both towards the technique of calculation and towards countless related _practices._

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#62.3) <span class="fac">[62\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/62.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-623}

<span class="series-number">300</span> With the words “_This_ number is the correct continuation of this sequence,” I can bring it about that in the future, he calls “correct continuation” what I call it. That is, I can teach him to continue a sequence (basic sequence) without using an expression of the ‘law of the sequence’; rather, to maintain a substrate for the meaning of algebraic rules, or something similar.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#62.4) <span class="fac">[62\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/62.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-624}

<span class="series-number">301</span> He must continue in this way _without reason_. But not because one cannot make the reason comprehensible to him, but because – in _this_ system – there is no reason. (“The chain of reasons has an end.”) And the _way_ (in “continue in this way”) is designated by a number, a value. For at _this_ stage, the expression of the rule is explained by the value, not the value by the rule.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#62.5) <span class="fac">[62\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/62.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-625}

<span class="series-number">302</span> For where it says “But _don’t_ you see…!”

the rule is of no use; it is what is explained, not what explains.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#62.6) <span class="fac">[62\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/62.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-626}

<span class="series-number">303</span> “He grasps the rule intuitively.” – But why the rule?

and not how he should continue now?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#62.7+63.1) <span class="fac">[62\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/62.webp),[63\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/63.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-627631}

<span class="series-number">304</span> “Has he only seen the right thing, that one of the infinitely many relations that I am trying to bring close to him – has he only grasped it once, then he will now effortlessly continue the series correctly. I admit, he can only guess this relation that I mean (guess it intuitively) – but if he succeeds, then the _game is won_.” – But this ‘right’ thing that I mean, does not even exist. The comparison is wrong. There is not, as it were, a cog that he has to grasp, the right machine that, once chosen, automatically carries him on. It could be that something like this happens in our brains, but that doesn’t interest us.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#63.2) <span class="fac">[63\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/63.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-632}

<span class="series-number">305</span> “Do the same!” But in doing so, I must point to the rule. He must therefore already have learned to _apply_ it. Because what else does its expression mean to him?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#63.3) <span class="fac">[63\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/63.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-633}

<span class="series-number">306</span> Guessing the meaning of the rule, grasping it intuitively, could only mean: guessing its _application_. And that cannot mean: guessing the _way_, the _rule_ of its application, and guessing is not even the issue here.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#63.4) <span class="fac">[63\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/63.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-634}

<span class="series-number">307</span> I could, for example, guess which continuation would please the other person (perhaps by looking at his face)! One could only guess at the application of the rule if one could already choose between different applications.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#63.5) <span class="fac">[63\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/63.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-635}

<span class="series-number">308</span> One could then also think that, instead of ‘guessing the application of the rule’, he _invents_ it. Well, what would that look like? – Should he, for example, say: “Let ‘following the rule + 1’ mean, for example, writing: 1, 1+1, 1+1+1, etc.”? But what does he mean by that? The “etc.” already presupposes mastery of a technique. Instead of “etc.”, he could also have said: “You know what I mean.” And his explanation would simply have been a _definition_ of the expression “following the rule + 1”. _That_ would have been his “invention”.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#64.1) <span class="fac">[64\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/64.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-641}

<span class="series-number">309</span> We copy the numbers from 1 to 100, for example, and _proceed, think_ in this way. I could say it this way: When I copy the numbers from 1 to 100, how do I know that I will obtain a sequence of numbers that is correct when counting? And _what_ is the control _for?_ Or how should I describe this important empirical fact? Should I say that experience teaches that I always count the same way? Or that no number is lost when copying? Or that the numbers stay on the paper as they are, even if I don’t look at them? Or _all_ these facts? Or should I say that we simply don’t get into trouble? Or that, as a rule, everything seems to be in order? _That’s_ how we think. _That’s_ how we act. _That’s_ how we talk about it.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#64.2) <span class="fac">[64\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/64.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-642}

<span class="series-number">310</span> Suppose you should describe how people learn to count (in the decimal system, for example). You describe what the teacher says and does, and how the student reacts. In what the teacher says and does, there will be, for example, words and gestures that are intended to encourage the student to continue a series; also words like “He can count now.” Should the description that I give of the process of teaching and learning include, in addition to the teacher’s words, my own judgment: that the student can now count, or: that the student now understands the system of number words? If I don’t include such a judgment in the description, is it incomplete? And if I include it, do I go beyond the mere description? – Can I refrain from those judgments with the justification: “_That is all that happens_”?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#64.3+65.1) <span class="fac">[64\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/64.webp),[65\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/65.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-643651}

<span class="series-number">311</span> Shouldn’t I rather ask: “What does the description actually do? What is its purpose?” – What constitutes a complete and an incomplete description, we certainly know in another context. Ask yourself: How are the expressions “complete” and “incomplete description” used? To reproduce a speech completely (or incompletely). Does this also include reproducing the tone of voice, the play of facial expressions, the authenticity or inauthenticity of the feelings, the intentions of the speaker, the effort of speaking? Whether this or that belongs to the complete description will depend on the purpose of the description, on what the recipient does with the description.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#65.2) <span class="fac">[65\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/65.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-652}

<span class="series-number">312</span> The expression “That is all that _happens_” demarcates what we call “happening”.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#65.3) <span class="fac">[65\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/65.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-653}

<span class="series-number">313</span> Here the temptation is overwhelming to say something more, even when everything has already been described. – Where does this urge come from? What analogy, what false interpretation creates it?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#65.4) <span class="fac">[65\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/65.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-654}

<span class="series-number">314</span> Here we encounter a remarkable and characteristic phenomenon in philosophical investigations: The difficulty – could I say – is not to find the solution, but to recognize something as the solution, which looks as if it were only a preliminary stage to it. “We have already said everything. – Not something that follows from it, but precisely _this_ is the solution!” I believe this is related to the fact that we wrongly expect an explanation; whereas a description is the solution to the difficulty if we correctly place it in our consideration. If we linger with it, not trying to go beyond it. The difficulty here is: to stop.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#65.5) <span class="fac">[65\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/65.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-655}

<span class="series-number">315</span> “Why do you ask for explanations? If these are given, you will still end up at an end. They cannot lead you further than you are now.”

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#65.6+66.1) <span class="fac">[65\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/65.webp),[66\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/66.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-656661}

<span class="series-number">316</span> One can use a red object as a sample for painting a _reddish_ white, or a reddish yellow (etc.) – but can one also use it as a sample for painting a blue-green hue, for example? – What if I saw someone, with all the external signs of accurate copying, ‘reproduce’ a red patch in blue-green? – I would say “I don’t know how he does it!” or even “I don’t know _what_ he is doing.” – But suppose he now ‘copied’ this tone of red in blue-green on various occasions, and other tones of red regularly in other blue-green tones – should I now say that he is copying, or that he is not copying? But what does it mean that I do not know “what he is doing”? Don’t I see what he is doing? – But I don’t see _into_ him. – Not this simile! If I see him copy red in red, what do I know? Do I know _how_ I do it? Of course, one says: I just paint the _same_ color. – But what if _he_ says, “And I am painting the fifth in relation to this color”? Do I see a special process of mediation when _I_ paint the ‘same’ color? Suppose I know him as an honest person; he reproduces a red through a blue-green, as I have described, but now _not_ always the same tone with the same, but sometimes with one tone, sometimes with another. – Should I say “I don’t know what he is doing”? – He does what I see – but _I_ would never do it; I don’t know why he does it; his way of acting ‘is incomprehensible to me’.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#66.2) <span class="fac">[66\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/66.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-662}

<span class="series-number">317</span> One could conceive of a negative _image_, that is, one that is meant to represent how Mr. N. does _not_ look (which would therefore be a bad portrait if it resembled Mr. N.).

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#66.3) <span class="fac">[66\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/66.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-663}

<span class="series-number">318</span> I cannot describe how a rule (in general) is to be used, except in the act of _teaching_ you, _training_ you to use a rule.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#66.4) <span class="fac">[66\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/66.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-664}

<span class="series-number">319</span> I can now, for example, record such an instruction in a talking film. The teacher will sometimes say, “That is correct.” If the student asks him, “Why?” – then he will give no answer, or rather no relevant answer, not even this: “Well, because we do so.”

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#66.5+67.1) <span class="fac">[66\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/66.webp),[67\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/67.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-665671}

<span class="series-number">320</span> Why do I not call the rules of cooking arbitrary, and why am I tempted to call the rules of grammar arbitrary? Because ‘cooking’ is defined by its purpose, while ‘speaking’ is not. That is why the use of language is, in a certain sense, autonomous, in a way that cooking and washing are not. Whoever follows rules other than the correct ones when cooking cooks badly; but whoever follows rules other than those of chess plays _a different game_; and whoever follows rules other than these grammatical rules does not speak something false, but something else.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#67.2) <span class="fac">[67\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/67.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-672}

<span class="series-number">321</span> If one adds a rule, concerning a word in the sentence, to the sentence, its sense does not change.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#67.3) <span class="fac">[67\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/67.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-673}

<span class="series-number">322</span> Language is not defined for us as a device that fulfills a specific purpose. Rather, “language” is, for me, a collective term, and I understand it to include the German language, the English language, and so on, as well as various systems of signs that have a greater or lesser affinity with these languages.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#67.4) <span class="fac">[67\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/67.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-674}

<span class="series-number">323</span> Our knowledge of many languages does not allow us to fully appreciate the philosophy that is embodied in the forms of each of them. At the same time, however, we are blind to the fact that we ourselves have strong prejudices for or against certain forms of expression; that this particular superposition of several languages also gives us an image.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#67.5) <span class="fac">[67\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/67.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-675}

<span class="series-number">324</span> Does the child only learn to speak, or also to think? Does it learn the sense of multiplication _before_ or _after_ multiplying?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#67.6) <span class="fac">[67\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/67.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-676}

<span class="series-number">325</span> How did I come to the concept of ‘sentence’, or the concept of ‘language’? Only through the languages that I have learned. – But they seem to have, in a certain sense, led me beyond themselves, because I am now able to construct a new language, e.g., to invent words. – So this construction is also part of the concept of language. But only if I want to define it that way.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#67.7) <span class="fac">[67\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/67.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-677}

<span class="series-number">326</span> The concept of a living being has the same indeterminacy as that of language.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#67.8) <span class="fac">[67\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/67.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-678}

<span class="series-number">327</span> Compare: inventing a game – inventing a language – inventing a machine.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#67.9) <span class="fac">[67\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/67.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-679}

<span class="series-number">328</span> The fact that this and that sentence has no sense is of importance in philosophy; but so is the fact that it sounds funny.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#68.1) <span class="fac">[68\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/68.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-681}

<span class="series-number">329</span> I make a plan not only to make myself understandable to others, but also to become clear about the matter myself. (That is, language is not only a means of communication.)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#68.2) <span class="fac">[68\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/68.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-682}

<span class="series-number">330</span> What does it mean to say: “That is a completely different game!” How do I use this sentence? As a communication? Well, perhaps as an introduction to a communication that lists the differences and explains their consequences. But also, to express that I will no longer participate, or that I will take a different position in the game.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#68.3) <span class="fac">[68\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/68.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-683}

<span class="series-number">331</span> One is tempted to justify the rules of grammar by means of sentences of the kind “But there really are four primary colors.” And the statement that the rules of grammar are arbitrary is directed against the possibility of this kind of justification, which is modeled on the justification of a sentence by reference to its verification. But can one not, in some sense, say that the grammar of color words characterizes the world as it actually is? One would like to say: Can I not, in fact, search in vain for a fifth primary color? Do we not group the primary colors together because they have a similarity; or at least the _colors_, in contrast to, for example, shapes or sounds, because they have a similarity? Or if I present this division of the world as the correct one, do I already have a preconceived idea in my head as a paradigm? From which I can then only say: “Yes, this is the way we look at things,” or “We simply want to create such a picture.” For if I say: “The primary colors do, after all, have a certain similarity with one another” – where do I get the concept of this similarity from? Is it not the case that, just as the concept ‘primary color’ is nothing other than ‘blue or red or green or yellow,’ the concept of that similarity is also only given by the four colors? Yes, are they not the same? – “Could one not also group red, green, and circular together?” – Why not?!

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#69.1) <span class="fac">[69\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/69.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-691}

<span class="series-number">332</span> Don’t believe that you hold the concept of color within you because you are looking at a colored object – however you are looking. (Just as little as you possess the concept of a negative number by having debts.)

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#69.2) <span class="fac">[69\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/69.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-692}

<span class="series-number">333</span> “Red is something specific,” that would have to mean something like: “_This_ is something specific” – whereby one points to something red. But in order for that to be understandable, one would already have to mean our _concept_ of ‘red,’ the use of that pattern.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#69.3) <span class="fac">[69\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/69.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-693}

<span class="series-number">334</span> 2136 I can obviously express an expectation at one time in the words “I expect a red circle,” and at another time, instead of the last two words, by means of the colored image of a red circle. But in this expression, two words, “red” and “circle,” do not correspond to two things. Thus, the expression of the second language is of _an entirely different kind._

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#69.4) <span class="fac">[69\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/69.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-694}

<span class="series-number">335</span> Besides this, there would also be a language in which ‘red circle’ is expressed by placing a circle and a red patch next to each other.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#69.5+70.1) <span class="fac">[69\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/69.webp),[70\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/70.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-695701}

<span class="series-number">336</span> If I now also have two signs at my disposal, the expression “red circle” & the colored image, or the image of the red circle, then the question would be: how is the one word assigned to the color, the other to the shape? For one seems to be able to say that the one word directs attention to the color, the other to the shape. But what does that mean? How can one translate these words into this image?

Or also: if the word ‘red’ brings a color to my memory, then it must be connected with a shape; how can I then abstract from the shape?

The important question here is never: how does he know what he should abstract from? but: how is that even possible? or: what does it mean?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#69.5+70.1.2) <span class="fac">[69\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/69.webp),[70\[1\].2](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/70.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-6957012}

<span class="series-number">337</span> Perhaps it will become clearer if one compares the two languages in which, in one, a red square and one with a circle on it (something like a black one on a white background) replace the words “red circle”; and in the other, instead, a red circle is painted. How does the translation proceed here? One looks, for example, first at the red square and chooses a red pen, then at the circle, and now makes a circle with this pen. It would be as if one first learned that the first square always determines the choice of the pencil, the second, what we are supposed to draw with it. The two squares thus belong to different parts of speech (something like a noun and a verb). But in the other language, there would be nothing that one could call two words _here_.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#70.2) <span class="fac">[70\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/70.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-702}

<span class="series-number">338</span> If someone said “Red is composite”—we couldn’t guess what he meant, what he intends to do with this sentence. But if he says, “This armchair is composite,” we may not immediately know what kind of composition he is talking about, but we can immediately think of more than one sense for his statement. What kind of fact is this that I have pointed out? In any case, it is an _important_ fact. – We are not familiar with any technique to which this sentence could refer.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#70.3) <span class="fac">[70\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/70.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-703}

<span class="series-number">339</span> We are describing here a language-game which we _cannot learn_.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#70.4) <span class="fac">[70\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/70.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-704}

<span class="series-number">340</span> “Then something quite different must be going on in him, something that we do not know.” – _This_ _shows us_ what we mean by saying that something is different from, or the same as, what is happening in us. This shows us _what_ we use to judge the inner processes.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#70.5) <span class="fac">[70\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/70.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-705}

<span class="series-number">341</span> Can you imagine what the red-green blind person sees? Can you paint the picture of the room as he sees it? Can he paint it as he sees it? Can I then paint how I see it? In what sense can I?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#70.6) <span class="fac">[70\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/70.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-706}

<span class="series-number">342</span> “Anyone who could see only gray, black, and white would have to be _given_ something so that he would know what red, green, etc., are.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#71.1) <span class="fac">[71\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/71.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-711}

<span class="series-number">343</span> And what would have to be given to him? Well, the colors. So, for example, _this,_ and _this,_ and _this_. (Imagine, for example, that colored models would have to be introduced into his brain, in addition to the merely gray and black ones.) But would this have to happen as a means to the end of future action? Or does this action already include these models? Do I mean: “Something would have to be given to him, because it is clear that he could not otherwise…” – or: His seeing behaviour _includes_ new components? Also: what would we _call_ an “explanation of seeing”? Should one say: Well, you know what “explanation” means; so use this concept here as well!

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#71.2) <span class="fac">[71\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/71.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-712}

<span class="series-number">344</span> Can I say: “Look at it! Then you will see that it cannot be explained.” – Or: “Drink the color red into yourself, and you will see that it cannot be represented by something else!” – And if the other person now agrees with me, does it show that he has absorbed the same thing as I have? – And what does our inclination to say this mean? Red appears to us to stand alone. Why? What is this appearance, this inclination _worth_? But one could ask: To what peculiarity of the concept does this inclination of ours point?

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#71.3) <span class="fac">[71\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/71.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-713}

<span class="series-number">345</span> Think of the sentence “Red is not a mixed color” and its function. The language-game with colors is characterized precisely by what we can do and what we cannot do.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#71.4) <span class="fac">[71\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/71.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-714}

<span class="series-number">346</span> “A reddish green does not exist” is related to the sentences that we use as axioms in mathematics.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#71.5) <span class="fac">[71\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/71.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-715}

<span class="series-number">347</span> The fact that we _calculate_ with certain concepts, but not with others, simply shows how different the conceptual tools are (how little reason we have to assume any uniformity here). Consider sentences about colors that are similar to mathematical ones, e.g.: Blue is darker than white. Also consider Goethe’s theory of colors.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#72.1) <span class="fac">[72\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/72.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-721}

<span class="series-number">348</span> “The possibility of agreement already presupposes _an_ agreement.” – Think of someone saying: “Knowing how to play chess is a kind of playing chess”!

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#72.2) <span class="fac">[72\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/72.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-722}

<span class="series-number">349</span> It is very difficult to describe thought paths when there are already so many well-trodden paths—whether your own or those of others—and not to end up on one of the established paths. It is difficult: to deviate _only a little_ from an old thought path.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#72.3) <span class="fac">[72\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/72.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-723}

<span class="series-number">350</span> “It is as if our concepts are conditioned by a framework of facts.” This meant: if you think of certain facts differently, if you describe them differently from how they are, then you can no longer imagine the application of certain concepts, because the rules of their application have no analogue under the new circumstances. – What I am saying, therefore, comes down to this: a law is given for people, and a lawyer may well be capable of drawing consequences for every case that usually occurs to him; the law therefore obviously has its use, a sense. Nevertheless, its validity presupposes all sorts of things; and if the nature of what it has to judge deviates completely from that of the ordinary person, then, for example, the decision as to whether he committed a deed with evil intention will not merely be difficult, but (simply) impossible.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#72.4) <span class="fac">[72\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/72.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-724}

<span class="series-number">351</span> “If people did not generally agree about the colors of things, if inconsistencies were not exceptions, then our concept of color could not exist.” No: – _there would not_ be our concept of color.

### [Ts-233a](/ts-233a/#72.5) <span class="fac">[72\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233a/72.webp)</span> {#ts-233a-725}

<span class="series-number">352</span> Do I want to say that certain facts are favorable or unfavorable to certain concept-formations? And does experience teach us this? It is an empirical fact that people change, alter their concepts when they become acquainted with new facts; when, as a result, what was previously important to them becomes unimportant, and vice versa. (One finds, for example: what used to be regarded as a difference in kind is actually _only_ a difference in degree.)

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#1.1) <span class="fac">[1\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/1.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-11}

<span class="series-number">353</span> But can one not say: “If there were only _one_ substance, would one have any use for the word ‘substance’”? But this means: the concept ‘substance’ presupposes the concept ‘difference of substance’. (As the concept of the chess king presupposes the concept of the chess move, or as the concept of _color_ presupposes the concept of _colors_.)

### [Ts-232](/ts-232/#712.6) <span class="fac">[712\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-232/712.webp)</span> {#ts-232-7126}

<span class="series-number">354</span> Between green and red, I want to say, there is a geometrical emptiness, not a physical one.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#1.2) <span class="fac">[1\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/1.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-12}

<span class="series-number">355</span> But does this correspond to something physical? I do not deny that. (And if it were merely our habit with _these_ concepts, with these language-games. But I do not say that that is the case.) If we teach a person the this and that technique by means of examples—that he then deals with a certain new case _in_ this way and not _in_ that way, or that he then hesitates, that for him this and not that is the ‘natural’ continuation, is already in itself a most important fact.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#1.3) <span class="fac">[1\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/1.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-13}

<span class="series-number">356</span> “But if I mean green by ‘bluish-yellow’, then I simply grasp this expression differently than in the original way. The original conception refers to another and precisely _unworkable_ path.” But what is the correct simile here? That of the physically unworkable path, or that of the non-existence of the path? Thus, the simile of physical, or mathematical impossibility?

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#1.4) <span class="fac">[1\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/1.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-14}

<span class="series-number">357</span> We have a system of colors as we have a system of numbers. Are the systems in _our_ nature, or in the nature of things? How is one to say it? – _Not_ in the nature of numbers or colors.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#1.5) <span class="fac">[1\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/1.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-15}

<span class="series-number">358</span> Does this system have something arbitrary about it? Yes and no. It is related to something arbitrary and to something non-arbitrary.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#1.6+2.1) <span class="fac">[1\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/1.webp),[2\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/2.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-1621}

<span class="series-number">359</span> It is clear that I want to recognize nothing but intermediate colors between red and green. (And whether people have always understood this, or only after experience and education, is irrelevant.)

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#2.2) <span class="fac">[2\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/2.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-22}

<span class="series-number">360</span> ‘a is between b and c, and closer to b than to c’ this is a characteristic relation between sensations of the same kind. That is to say, there is, for example, a language-game with the command “Generate a sensation between _this_ and _this_, and closer to the first than to the second!” And also: “Name two sensations between which _this_ lies.”

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#2.3) <span class="fac">[2\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/2.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-23}

<span class="series-number">361</span> And it is important that, for example, with _gray_ one will get “black and white” as the answer; with _violet_ “blue and red”, with _pink_ “red and white”, etc.; but _not_ with _olive-green_ “red and green”.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#2.4) <span class="fac">[2\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/2.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-24}

<span class="series-number">362</span> These people know a reddish-green. – “But there _is_ no such thing!” – What a strange sentence. – (How do you know?)

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#2.5) <span class="fac">[2\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/2.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-25}

<span class="series-number">363</span> Let us say it like this: must these people notice the discrepancy? Perhaps they are too dull for that. And then again: perhaps not. –

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#2.6) <span class="fac">[2\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/2.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-26}

<span class="series-number">364</span> But doesn’t nature have anything to say here?! Yes—but it makes itself heard in another way. “Somewhere you will run into existence and non-existence!” But this means _facts_, not concepts.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#2.7) <span class="fac">[2\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/2.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-27}

<span class="series-number">365</span> It is a fact of the greatest importance that a color that we are inclined to call (e.g.) “reddish-yellow” can actually be produced (in various ways) by mixing red and yellow. And that we are not able to recognize a color that has been created by mixing red and green as one that can be produced in this way. (But what does “without further ado” mean here?)

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#2.8+3.1) <span class="fac">[2\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/2.webp),[3\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/3.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-2831}

<span class="series-number">366</span> Confusion of tastes: I say “This is sweet”, the other says “This is sour”, etc. Someone comes along and says: “You all have no idea what you are talking about. You no longer know what you once called a taste.” What would be the sign that we still know it? [Related to a question about a confusion in arithmetic.]

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#3.2) <span class="fac">[3\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/3.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-32}

<span class="series-number">367</span> But couldn't we also play a language-game in this ‘confusion’? – But is it still the earlier one? – (This refers to what Frege, and occasionally Ramsey, said about recognition as a condition of symbolization. What is the criterion for me having correctly recognized the color? Something like the experience of pleasure upon recognition?)

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#3.2.2) <span class="fac">[3\[2\].2](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/3.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-322}

<span class="series-number">368</span> Let us imagine people who express an intermediate color, such as a shade between red & yellow, through a kind of binary decimal fraction, like this: R, LLRL, etc., where, for example, yellow is on the right side & red on the left. – These people would learn, even in kindergarten, to describe color shades in this way, to select colors according to these descriptions, to mix them, etc. They would relate to us in much the same way as people with perfect pitch relate to people who lack it. _They can do_ what we cannot do. Questionable.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#3.3) <span class="fac">[3\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/3.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-33}

<span class="series-number">369</span> And here one would like to say: “Is that conceivable? Yes, that _behaviour_ probably is! but also the inner process, the experience of color?” And what one should say in answer to such a question is hard to see. Could those who do not have perfect pitch have suspected that there must also be people with perfect pitch?

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#3.4) <span class="fac">[3\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/3.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-34}

<span class="series-number">370</span> The brilliance, or the reflection: When a child paints, it will never paint this. Yes, it is almost hard to believe that it can be represented by ordinary oil or watercolors.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#3.5+4.1) <span class="fac">[3\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/3.webp),[4\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/4.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-3541}

<span class="series-number">371</span> What would a society of only deaf people look like? What about a society of ‘mentally deficient’ people? _An important question!_ That is, what would a society look like that never played many of our usual language-games?

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#4.2) <span class="fac">[4\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/4.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-42}

<span class="series-number">372</span> The mentally deficient are imagined as degenerate, essentially incomplete, as if tattered. Thus, as disorder, instead of a more primitive order (which would be a much more productive way of looking at things). We do not see a _society_ of such people.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#4.3) <span class="fac">[4\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/4.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-43}

<span class="series-number">373</span> Other, albeit related, concepts could seem _very_ strange to us, namely deviations from the familiar _in an unfamiliar direction_.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#4.4) <span class="fac">[4\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/4.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-44}

<span class="series-number">374</span> Well-defined concepts would require a uniformity of behaviour. But where I am _certain_, the other is uncertain. And that is a fact of nature.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#4.5) <span class="fac">[4\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/4.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-45}

<span class="series-number">375</span> These are the fixed rails on which all our thinking proceeds, and therefore also our judging and acting.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#4a.1) <span class="fac">[4a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/4a.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-4a1}

<span class="series-number">376</span> There, for example, where there is only rarely a type, the concept of this type is not formed. People do not recognize this syndrome as a unity.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#4a.1.2) <span class="fac">[4a\[1\].2](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/4a.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-4a12}

<span class="series-number">377</span> They do not form a picture of it & recognize it from case to case.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#4.6) <span class="fac">[4\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/4.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-46}

<span class="series-number">378</span> Must the concept of modesty, or boastfulness, be known everywhere there are modest and boastful people? Perhaps they do not care about this distinction there. Some differences are unimportant to us, and could be important to us.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#4.7) <span class="fac">[4\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/4.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-47}

<span class="series-number">379</span> And others have concepts that cut across our concepts.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#4a.8) <span class="fac">[4a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/4a.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-4a8}

<span class="series-number">380</span> A tribe has two concepts, related to our ‘pain’. One is applied in cases of visible injuries and is associated with care, compassion, etc. The other they apply to stomach pains, for example, and it is associated with amusement at the complaining person. “But do they not really notice the similarity?” – Do we have a concept everywhere that a similarity exists? The question is: Is the similarity _important_? And must it be important to them? And why shouldn’t their concept cut across our concept of ‘pain’?

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#4a.9) <span class="fac">[4a\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/4a.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-4a9}

<span class="series-number">381</span> But doesn’t this mean that he overlooks something that is there? – He does not take note of it; and why should he? – But then his concept is fundamentally different from ours. – **Fundamentally** different?

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#5.2) <span class="fac">[5\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/5.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-52}

<span class="series-number">382</span> In philosophy, one must not cut short a mental malady. It must run its natural course, & the _slow_ healing is the most important thing. (Hence, mathematicians are such poor philosophers.)

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#5.3) <span class="fac">[5\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/5.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-53}

<span class="series-number">383</span> Imagine that the people of a tribe were raised from an early age to show _no_ emotional expression. It is something childish for them, which should be discarded. The training is strict. One doesn't talk about ‘pain’; let alone in the form of a conjecture “Perhaps he does have…”. If someone complains, they are laughed at or punished. The suspicion of pretending does not even exist. Complaining is, as it were, already pretending.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#5.4) <span class="fac">[5\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/5.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-54}

<span class="series-number">384</span> “Pretending,” those people might say, “what a ridiculous concept!” (As if one distinguished a murder with _one_ bullet from one with three bullets.)

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#6a.1) <span class="fac">[6a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/6a.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-6a1}

<span class="series-number">385</span> Complaining is already so bad that it eclipses the even worse act of _pretending_.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#6a.1.2) <span class="fac">[6a\[1\].2](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/6a.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-6a12}

<span class="series-number">386</span> One can no longer see one disgrace behind the other.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#5.5) <span class="fac">[5\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/5.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-55}

<span class="series-number">387</span> I mean: a completely different upbringing than ours could also be the basis for completely different concepts.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#5.6) <span class="fac">[5\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/5.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-56}

<span class="series-number">388</span> Because life would be different here. – What interests us would not interest _them_. Other concepts would no longer be unimaginable. Yes, _essentially_ different concepts can only be imagined in this way.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#5.7+6.1) <span class="fac">[5\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/5.webp),[6\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/6.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-5761}

<span class="series-number">389</span> One could simply teach him to mimic pain (e.g., not with the intention of deceiving). But could it be taught to everyone? I mean: he could probably learn to produce certain crude signs of pain without ever, from his own perspective, giving a more refined imitation (talent for language). (One could perhaps teach a clever dog a kind of pain-howl; but it would never come to a conscious imitation in him.)

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#6.2) <span class="fac">[6\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/6.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-62}

<span class="series-number">390</span> ‘These people would have nothing human about them.’ Why? – We could not possibly communicate with them. Not even as we can with a dog. We could not find ourselves in them. And yet, there could be such beings, otherwise human.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#6.3) <span class="fac">[6\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/6.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-63}

<span class="series-number">391</span> I want (actually) to say that the mental scruples originate in instinct (have their roots there). Or also: the language-game does not originate in _reflection._ Reflection is part of the language-game. And the concept is therefore at home in the language-game.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#6.3.2) <span class="fac">[6\[3\].2](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/6.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-632}

<span class="series-number">392</span> ‘A heap of sand’ is a term with an imprecise boundary – but why doesn’t one use a term with a precise boundary instead? – Is the reason to be found in the nature of heaps? What phenomenon is it whose nature determines our concept?

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#6.4) <span class="fac">[6\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/6.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-64}

<span class="series-number">393</span> One can easily imagine events and work them out in all details, which, if we were to see them happen, would lead us to make completely wrong judgments. If I were to see a completely new environment instead of the familiar one in front of my window, if things and animals were to behave in ways they have never behaved before, I might say, “I have gone mad”; but that would only be an expression of the fact that I give up trying to understand. And the same thing could happen to me in mathematics. It might _seem_ to me that I am always making calculation errors, so that no solution seems reliable to me. But what is important for me about this is that there is no sharp boundary between such a state and the normal one. It is connected with the concept of ‘knowledge’.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#8a.1) <span class="fac">[8a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/8a.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-8a1}

<span class="series-number">394</span> What would it mean to be mistaken in that he has a soul, consciousness? and what would it mean that I am mistaken and have none myself? What would it mean to say “I am not conscious”? – – But don’t I know that consciousness is in me? – So I know it, and yet the assertion that it is so has no purpose? And how remarkable that one can learn to communicate with other people on this subject!

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#8a.2) <span class="fac">[8a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/8a.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-8a2}

<span class="series-number">395</span> One can make oneself unconscious; but can one also become _conscious_?

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#7.2) <span class="fac">[7\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/7.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-72}

<span class="series-number">396</span> What if someone seriously told me they (really) didn't know whether they were dreaming or awake? – Is it possible for this situation to exist: someone says, “I think I'm dreaming now”; they really wake up soon afterward, remember that utterance in the dream, and say, “So I was right!” – This narrative can only _mean_: someone dreamed that they said they were dreaming. Suppose an unconscious person said (perhaps during anesthesia), “I am conscious” – would we say, “He must know”? And if someone spoke in their sleep and said, “I am sleeping,” – would we say, “He is absolutely right”? Does someone tell an untruth when they say to me, “I am not conscious”? (And the truth when they say it while unconscious? And how about when a parrot says, “I don’t understand a word,” or a gramophone says, “I am just a machine”?)

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#7.3) <span class="fac">[7\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/7.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-73}

<span class="series-number">397</span> Suppose, in a daydream, I were to say “I am merely fantasizing”; would that be _true_? Suppose I were to write such a fantasy, or story, a fantasized dialogue, and in it I say “I am fantasizing”—but, if I write it down, how does it show that these words are words of fantasy and that I have not stepped out of the fantasy? Wouldn’t it indeed be possible that the dreamer, as it were stepping out of the dream, speaks in his sleep and says “I am dreaming”? It would probably be conceivable that such a language-game existed. This is connected with the problem of ‘meaning’. For I can write in a dialogue of a play “I am healthy” and thus not _mean_ it, even though it is also true. The words belong to this language-game and not to that one.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#7.4) <span class="fac">[7\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/7.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-74}

<span class="series-number">398</span> ‘True’ and ‘False’ in dreams. I dream that it is raining and that I say “It is raining” – – on the other hand: I dream that I say “I am dreaming.”

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#7.5) <span class="fac">[7\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/7.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-75}

<span class="series-number">399</span> Does the verb “to dream” have a present tense? How does a person learn to use it?

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#6.5+7.1) <span class="fac">[6\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/6.webp),[7\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/7.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-6571}

<span class="series-number">400</span> Suppose I had an _experience_, similar to waking up, and found myself in a different environment, with people assuring me that I had been asleep. Suppose further that I maintain that I did not dream, but lived in some way outside my sleeping body. What function does this assertion have?

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#8.1) <span class="fac">[8\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/8.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-81}

<span class="series-number">401</span> “‘I am conscious,’ that is an assertion for which no doubt is possible.” Why shouldn’t that say the same thing as this: “‘I am conscious’ is not a sentence”? One could also say: What harm is there in someone saying that “I am conscious” is an assertion that admits of no doubt? How can I contradict him? Suppose someone said this to me – why shouldn’t I get used to not answering him anything, instead of, say, starting an argument? Why shouldn’t I treat his words as if they were his whistling or humming?

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#8.2) <span class="fac">[8\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/8.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-82}

<span class="series-number">402</span> “Nothing is as certain as that consciousness belongs to me.” Why shouldn’t I then just leave it at that? This certainty is like a great force, the point of application of which does not move; which, therefore, does no work.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#8.3) <span class="fac">[8\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/8.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-83}

<span class="series-number">403</span> Remember: most people say that one doesn’t feel anything during anesthesia. But some do say: one _could_ still feel something, but simply forgets it completely. So if there are some here who doubt and some for whom no doubt arises, then perhaps this certainty could also exist much more generally.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#8.4) <span class="fac">[8\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/8.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-84}

<span class="series-number">404</span> Or could the doubt take on other, and much less indefinite forms, than in our world of thoughts?

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#8.5) <span class="fac">[8\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/8.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-85}

<span class="series-number">405</span> No one but a philosopher would say “I know that I have two hands”; but one can say: I am not in a position to doubt that I have two hands.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#8.5.2) <span class="fac">[8\[5\].2](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/8.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-852}

<span class="series-number">406</span> “Knowledge,” however, is not usually used in this sense. “I know how much 97 × 78 is.” “I know that 97 × 78 is 432.” In the first case, I tell someone that I can do something, that I possess something; in the second, I simply assert that 97 × 78 is 432. Doesn’t the sentence “97 × 78 is definitely 432!” not mean, _I know_ that it is so? The first sentence is not an arithmetical one, nor can an arithmetical sentence replace it; in place of the second, one could use an arithmetical sentence.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#8.6) <span class="fac">[8\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/8.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-86}

<span class="series-number">407</span> Can someone _believe_ that 25 × 25 = 625? What does it mean to believe that? How does it show that he believes it?

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#9.1) <span class="fac">[9\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/9.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-91}

<span class="series-number">408</span> But is there not a phenomenon of knowledge, so to speak, quite apart from the sense of the words “I know”? Is it not remarkable that a person can _know_ something, as if the fact were, in a way, in himself? – But that is just a false picture. – For, one says, knowledge is only what it is when it actually corresponds to what he says. But that is not enough. It must not only happen to be so by chance. He must, after all, know that he knows; knowledge is, after all, his own state of mind; he cannot, except through a special delusion, be in doubt, or be wrong about it. If, then, the knowledge _that_ it is so is only knowledge if it _really_ is so; and if the knowledge is in him, so that he cannot be mistaken in whether it is knowledge; then he is (therefore) also infallible in the fact that it is as the knowledge knows it; and thus the fact that he knows must also be in him, as the knowledge is. And that certainly points to a certain kind of use of “I know.” “I know that it is so” then means: It is so, or I am crazy. Therefore: if I say, without lying, “I know that it is so,” then I can only be wrong through a special delusion.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#9.2) <span class="fac">[9\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/9.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-92}

<span class="series-number">409</span> How does it come about that doubt does not succumb to arbitrariness? – And if it is so, could not a child, through his curious disposition, doubt everything?

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#9.3) <span class="fac">[9\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/9.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-93}

<span class="series-number">410</span> One can only begin to doubt when one has learned something certain; just as one can only make mistakes in calculation when one has learned to calculate. Then, however, it is involuntary.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#9.4) <span class="fac">[9\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/9.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-94}

<span class="series-number">411</span> Imagine that a child is particularly intelligent, so intelligent that one can teach him the doubtfulness of the existence of all things right away. He thus learns from the beginning: “That is probably a chair.” And how does he now learn the question: “Is that really a chair?” –

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#9.5) <span class="fac">[9\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/9.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-95}

<span class="series-number">412</span> Am I doing child psychology? – I connect the concept of teaching with the concept of meaning.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#9.6+10.1) <span class="fac">[9\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/9.webp),[10\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/10.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-96101}

<span class="series-number">413</span> Let one person be a convinced realist, and the other a convinced idealist, and let them teach their children accordingly. In something as important as the existence, or non-existence, of the external world, they do not want to teach their children anything false. What will they then teach them? Also, to say “There are physical objects,” or the opposite? If one does not believe in fairies, he does not need to teach his children “There are no fairies,” but he can simply refrain from teaching them the word “fairy.” On what occasion should they say “There is…” or “There is not…”? Only when they meet people with opposing beliefs.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#10.2) <span class="fac">[10\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/10.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-102}

<span class="series-number">414</span> But the idealist will still teach the children the word “chair,” because he wants to teach them to do certain things, e.g., to fetch a chair. Where, then, will what the idealistically raised children say differ from what the realistically raised children say? Will the difference not just be that of battle cries?

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#10.3) <span class="fac">[10\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/10.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-103}

<span class="series-number">415</span> Doesn’t the “game” of “that is probably a…” begin with disappointment? And could the initial attitude be one of potential disappointment?

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#10.4) <span class="fac">[10\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/10.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-104}

<span class="series-number">416</span> “So, must one first teach him a false sense of certainty?” In their language-game, it is not yet a question of certainty or uncertainty. Remember, they are learning to _do_ something.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#10.3.2) <span class="fac">[10\[3\].2](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/10.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-1032}

<span class="series-number">417</span> The language-game “What is that?” – “A chair.” – is not the same as: “What do you think that is?” – “It is probably a chair.”

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#10.5) <span class="fac">[10\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/10.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-105}

<span class="series-number">418</span> Teaching a child “This seems red” at the beginning makes no sense. He must be able to say it spontaneously, once he has learned what “red” means, i.e., the technique of using the word.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#10.6) <span class="fac">[10\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/10.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-106}

<span class="series-number">419</span> The basis of every explanation is training. (This is something educators should consider.)

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#10.7) <span class="fac">[10\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/10.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-107}

<span class="series-number">420</span> “It seems red to me.” – “And what is red?” – “_Like this_.” One must then point to the correct paradigm.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#11.1) <span class="fac">[11\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/11.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-111}

<span class="series-number">421</span> If he first learns the color names, – what is he taught? Well, he learns, for example, to say “red” when he sees something red. – Is this a correct description, or should it have said: “He learns to ‘call it red,’ _which is also what we_ ‘call red’”? – Both descriptions are correct. How does this differ from the language-game “How does it strike you?”? One could teach him the color words by having him look at white objects through colored glasses. But what I teach him must be a _skill_. _He can_ now, on command, bring something red; or arrange objects according to their colors. But what is something red?

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#11.2) <span class="fac">[11\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/11.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-112}

<span class="series-number">422</span> Why doesn’t one teach the child the language-game “It seems red to me” right away? Because he is not yet able to understand the finer distinction between appearance and reality?

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#11.3) <span class="fac">[11\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/11.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-113}

<span class="series-number">423</span> The red visual sensation is a new _concept_.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#11.4) <span class="fac">[11\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/11.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-114}

<span class="series-number">424</span> The language-game that we then teach him is: “It seems to me…, it seems to you…” In the first language-game, a person does not appear as a perceiving subject.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#11.5) <span class="fac">[11\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/11.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-115}

<span class="series-number">425</span> You are giving the language-game a new articulation. But this does not mean that it will always be used.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#11.6) <span class="fac">[11\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/11.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-116}

<span class="series-number">426</span> The inner looking at the sensation – what connection should it establish between word and sensation; what is this connection supposed to serve? Was I taught _that_ when I learned to use this sentence, to think this thought? (To think it is something I would have to learn.) We also learn to direct our attention to things, and to sensations. We learn to observe and describe the observation. But how is I taught this; how is my ‘inner activity’ controlled in this case? According to what is it judged whether I have really paid attention?

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#11.7) <span class="fac">[11\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/11.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-117}

<span class="series-number">427</span> “The chair is the same, whether I look at it or not” – this does not _have_ to be true. People often become embarrassed when they are looked at. “The chair continues to exist, whether I look at it or not.” This could be treated as an empirical proposition, or it could be understood grammatically. One can also simply think of the conceptual difference between sense impression and object in this connection.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#12.1) <span class="fac">[12\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/12.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-121}

<span class="series-number">428</span> But isn't the agreement of people essential to the game? Must one who learns it therefore not first know the meaning of “same,” and doesn’t that also presuppose agreement? etc.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#12.2) <span class="fac">[12\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/12.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-122}

<span class="series-number">429</span> You say, “_This_ is red,” but how will it be decided whether you are right? Isn’t it decided by the agreement of people? – But do I rely on this agreement in my judgments about colors? Does it happen _like this_: I have a number of people look at an object; each of them has one of a certain group of words (the so-called color words) occur to them; if the word “red,” for example, occurs to the majority of the observers (I don’t necessarily have to be among that majority), then the object is given the predicate “red.” Such a technique could certainly have its importance.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#12.3) <span class="fac">[12\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/12.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-123}

<span class="series-number">430</span> The _color words_ are taught _like this_: “This is red,” for example. Our language-game, of course, only comes about when there is a certain agreement, but the concept of agreement does not enter into the language-game. If the agreement were complete, its concept could be completely unknown.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#12.4) <span class="fac">[12\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/12.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-124}

<span class="series-number">431</span> Does the _agreement_ of people determine what is red? Is this decided by appealing to the majority? Were we taught to determine the color _so_?

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#12.5) <span class="fac">[12\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/12.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-125}

<span class="series-number">432</span> I am simply describing the language-game “Bring something red” to someone who can already play it himself. I could only teach it to others. (Relativity.)

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#12.6+13.1) <span class="fac">[12\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/12.webp),[13\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/13.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-126131}

<span class="series-number">433</span> “What I perceive is _**this**_” – and then follows a form of _**description**_. One could also explain the word “This” as follows: Let us imagine a direct transmission of the experience! – But what is our criterion for the fact that the experience has actually been transmitted? “Well, he just has what I have.” – But how does he _‘have’_ it?

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#13.2) <span class="fac">[13\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/13.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-132}

<span class="series-number">434</span> What does it mean to “designate a sensation with a word, to name it”? Is there nothing to investigate here? Imagine you come from a language-game with spatial objects – and now it turns out that _sensations_ are also being named. Wouldn’t that be as if, first, one spoke of the transfer of possession, and then also of the transfer of pleasure in possession, or of pride in possession. Must we not learn something new here? Something new that we also call “transfer.”

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#13.3) <span class="fac">[13\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/13.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-133}

<span class="series-number">435</span> The description of the subjectively seen is closely or distantly related to the description of an object, but it does not function as a description of an object for that reason. How does one compare visual sensations? How do I compare my visual sensations with those of another?

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#13.4) <span class="fac">[13\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/13.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-134}

<span class="series-number">436</span> “Verifying by inspection” is a completely misleading expression. It says, namely, that first a process, the inspection, takes place, and that would be comparable to looking through a microscope, or to the process of turning one’s head _to see something._ And that then seeing necessarily takes place. One could speak of “seeing by turning” or “seeing by looking.” But then the turning (or looking) is a process external to seeing, which only interests us (_therefore_) in a practical way. What one wants to say is: “seeing by seeing.”

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#14.2) <span class="fac">[14\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/14.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-142}

<span class="series-number">437</span> The reasons why we believe a sentence are irrelevant to the question of what it is that we believe; but not the reasons, which are grammatically related to the sentence and tell us who it is.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#14.3) <span class="fac">[14\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/14.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-143}

<span class="series-number">438</span> It is quite common for the meaning of an expression to fluctuate in such a way that a phenomenon is sometimes regarded as a symptom and sometimes as a criterion of a _fact_. And in most cases, this shift in meaning goes unnoticed. In science, it is common to make phenomena that allow for precise measurements the defining criteria of an expression; and one is then inclined to believe that the true meaning has now been _found_. A great deal of confusion has arisen in this way. There are, for example, degrees of pleasure, but it is nonsensical to speak of measuring pleasure. It is true that in certain cases, a measurable phenomenon takes the place of one that was previously immeasurable. The word that designates this place then changes its meaning, and its old meaning becomes more or less obsolete. One then consoles oneself with the thought that one concept is more precise, the other less precise; and one does not notice that in each particular case, a different relationship exists between the ‘precise’ and the ‘imprecise’. This is the old mistake of not examining the particular cases.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#14.4+15.1) <span class="fac">[14\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/14.webp),[15\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/15.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-144151}

<span class="series-number">439</span> Sufficient evidence gradually fades into insufficient evidence, without having definite boundaries. Should I say that the complicated essence and diversity of human cases form a natural basis for this conceptualization? Thus, with a much smaller diversity, a sharply defined conceptualization would naturally seem possible. And why does it seem so difficult to imagine the simplified case?

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#14.4+15.1.2) <span class="fac">[14\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/14.webp),[15\[1\].2](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/15.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-1441512}

<span class="series-number">440</span> How would one imagine a complete catalog of rules for the use of a word? – What does one understand by a complete catalog of rules for the use of a figure in a game of chess? Couldn't one always construct cases of doubt in which the normal catalog of rules does not decide? Consider a question of this sort: how is it possible to determine who made the last move if the reliability of the players' memories is doubted? The traffic regulations in the streets permit and prohibit certain actions of drivers and pedestrians; but they do not attempt to guide all their movements through regulations. And it would be senseless to speak of an ‘ideal’ traffic code that would do this; we would not even know what to imagine under this ideal. If someone wishes to make the traffic code stricter in certain respects, that does not mean that he wishes to bring it closer to such an ideal.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#14.4+15.1.3) <span class="fac">[14\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/14.webp),[15\[1\].3](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/15.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-1441513}

<span class="series-number">441</span> Consider this sentence as well: “The rules of a game can probably allow for a certain degree of freedom, but they must _nevertheless_ be quite specific rules.” This is like saying “One can allow a person a certain degree of freedom of movement by means of four walls, but the walls must be completely rigid” – and that is not true. “Now, the walls can probably be elastic, but then they have a very specific elasticity.” – What does that really say? It seems to say that one must be able to specify this elasticity, but that is not true either.

“The wall always has _a specific_ elasticity – whether one knows it or not”: this is essentially an acknowledgement of a form of expression. Of those who make use of the _form_ of an ideal of precision, as if it were a parameter of representation.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#16.1) <span class="fac">[16\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/16.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-161}

<span class="series-number">442</span> 104 The profession of a form of expression, when it is expressed in the guise of a sentence that deals with the _objects_ (instead of the sign) must be ‘_a priori_’. For its opposite becomes truly unthinkable, insofar as it corresponds to a form of thought, a form of expression, that we have excluded.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#16.2) <span class="fac">[16\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/16.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-162}

<span class="series-number">443</span> Imagine that people used to point to objects in such a way that they traced a circle around the object in the air with their finger; then one might imagine a philosopher saying: “Every _thing_ is round; for the table looks _so_, the stove _so_, the lamp _so_,” and so on, by tracing a circle around each thing.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#16.3+17.1) <span class="fac">[16\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/16.webp),[17\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/17.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-163171}

<span class="series-number">444</span> We now have a _theory_; a ‘dynamic’ theory* of the sentence, of language, but it does not appear to us as a theory. For what is characteristic of such a theory is that it looks at a particular, clearly evident, case, & says: “_This_ shows how it is in general; this case is the prototype of _all_ cases.”‒ ‒ We have arrived at a form of representation that seems _plausible_ to us. But it is as if we have now seen something that lies _beneath_ the surface.

Freud speaks of a ‘dynamic’ theory of dreams. The tendency to generalize from the clear case seems to have strict warrant in logic: one seems to be justified in concluding with _full_ warrant: “If _one_ sentence is a picture, then every sentence must be a picture, for they must all be essentially the same.” For we are under the illusion that the sublime, essential thing about our investigation consists in its grasping _one_ all-encompassing essence.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#17.2) <span class="fac">[17\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/17.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-172}

<span class="series-number">445</span> How can one understand the sentence _now_, if the analysis is supposed to show _what_ one actually understands? – The idea of understanding as a peculiar mental process comes into play here. Don’t think of understanding as a ‘psychological process’ at all: because _that_ is the way of speaking that confuses you. Instead, ask yourself: in what kind of case, under what kind of circumstances, do we say “now I know what to do” – when the formula occurs to us?

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#17.3) <span class="fac">[17\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/17.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-173}

<span class="series-number">446</span> It is that way of speaking which prevents us from seeing the facts impartially. Consider the pronunciation of a word through the form of its spelling: how easily can one be persuaded that two words – e.g. “for” & “lead” – have different sounds in everyday use – because one pronounces them differently when one’s attention is directed to the difference in their spelling. This can be compared to the opinion that a violinist with a fine ear always plays f slightly higher than eis. Consider such cases! – _So_ it can happen that the means of representation creates an _**illusion**_. Not that we **must** find a specific mental process, because the verb “understand” is there & because one says: understanding is a mental activity.

### [Ts-233b](/ts-233b/#18.1) <span class="fac">[18\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ts-233b/18.webp)</span> {#ts-233b-181}

<span class="series-number">447</span> The unrest in philosophy, one might say, comes from the fact that we look at philosophy the wrong way, that we see it the wrong way, namely as if it were divided into (endless) longitudinal strips, instead of (limited) transverse strips. The change in perspective is the _greatest_ difficulty. We want, as it were, to grasp the unlimited strip, and complain that it is not possible piece by piece. Of course not, if one understands a piece as an endless longitudinal strip.
